# Daily Summary: 2025-07-26

### Dual-branch Prompting for Multimodal Machine Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17588v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Wang, Zhendong Yang, Liansong Zong, Xiaobo Zhang, Dexian Wang, Ji Zhang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Machine Translation (MMT) typically enhances text-only translation by incorporating aligned visual features. Despite the remarkable progress, state-of-the-art MMT approaches often rely on paired image-text inputs at inference and are sensitive to irrelevant visual noise, which limits their robustness and practical applicability. To address these issues, we propose D2P-MMT, a diffusion-based dual-branch prompting framework for robust vision-guided translation. Specifically, D2P-MMT requires only the source text and a reconstructed image generated by a pre-trained diffusion model, which naturally filters out distracting visual details while preserving semantic cues. During training, the model jointly learns from both authentic and reconstructed images using a dual-branch prompting strategy, encouraging rich cross-modal interactions. To bridge the modality gap and mitigate training-inference discrepancies, we introduce a distributional alignment loss that enforces consistency between the output distributions of the two branches. Extensive experiments on the Multi30K dataset demonstrate that D2P-MMT achieves superior translation performance compared to existing state-of-the-art approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces D2P-MMT, a novel diffusion-based dual-branch prompting framework for multimodal machine translation (MMT). It addresses the limitations of traditional MMT approaches that rely on paired image-text inputs at inference and are sensitive to irrelevant visual noise.  D2P-MMT uses only the source text and a reconstructed image generated by a pre-trained diffusion model during inference.  The system trains on both authentic and reconstructed images using a dual-branch prompting strategy, encouraging rich cross-modal interactions and employing a distributional alignment loss to ensure consistency between the two branches.  Experiments on the Multi30K dataset demonstrate improved translation performance compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components that contribute to its overall novelty:

*   **Diffusion-based Image Reconstruction:** The use of a pre-trained diffusion model to reconstruct images from the source text to filter out irrelevant visual details is a significant departure from directly using authentic images. This addresses a key limitation of previous MMT systems.
*   **Dual-Branch Prompting:** The dual-branch architecture, processing both authentic and reconstructed images during training, is also a novel aspect. It allows the model to learn robust features adaptable to varying visual inputs. The textual prompt guiding the image reconstruction further enhances the visual encoding.
*   **Distributional Alignment Loss (KL Divergence):** The use of KL divergence to align the output distributions from the two branches enhances consistency and encourages the model to learn representations that generalize across different visual inputs (authentic and reconstructed).
*   **Multi-Level Visual Prompt Enhancement Module:** The module enhances visual representations by extracting both local and global information in images.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the robustness and practical applicability of MMT systems.  By removing the need for paired image-text inputs at inference and reducing sensitivity to irrelevant visual noise, D2P-MMT could be deployed in real-world scenarios where visual information might be unreliable or unavailable. The reported performance improvements on the Multi30K dataset further validate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
However, several aspects need critical scrutiny:

*   **Dataset Limitations:** The Multi30K dataset, while widely used, is relatively small and contains simplistic vocabulary. The improvements demonstrated on Multi30K may not directly translate to larger, more complex datasets. The paper acknowledges this limitation but it needs to be stressed, since this issue is quite significant for practical applications.

*   **Dependence on Stable Diffusion:** While using a pre-trained Stable Diffusion model avoids training a text-to-image model from scratch, it also introduces a dependency on the performance and biases of that specific model. The reconstructive process of Stable Diffusion may alter information within images used during inference. The paper acknowledges the risks associated with Stable Diffusion.

*   **Ablation Studies and Analysis:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of individual components, but a more detailed analysis of the types of errors the system is able to correct or avoid compared to baseline methods would strengthen the claims. The qualitative results need to be expanded to better visually display the benefits.

*   **Comparison to SOTA:** Compared to previous SOTA MMT results, the performance gains of D2P-MMT do not seem significant.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.
The paper demonstrates a solid, novel approach to MMT that addresses a significant limitation of previous systems. The use of diffusion models for image reconstruction and the dual-branch prompting framework are innovative. The experimental results, while limited by the Multi30K dataset, are promising.
The major weakness is the relatively small margin of improvement of results on Multi30K compared to the state-of-the-art MMT, and the dependence on Stable Diffusion. Further research on larger, more complex datasets and exploring alternative image reconstruction techniques is needed to fully realize the potential of the approach.

### Vision Transformer attention alignment with human visual perception in aesthetic object evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17616v1)
- **Authors**: Miguel Carrasco, César González-Martín, José Aranda, Luis Oliveros
- **Abstract**: Visual attention mechanisms play a crucial role in human perception and aesthetic evaluation. Recent advances in Vision Transformers (ViTs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in computer vision tasks, yet their alignment with human visual attention patterns remains underexplored, particularly in aesthetic contexts. This study investigates the correlation between human visual attention and ViT attention mechanisms when evaluating handcrafted objects. We conducted an eye-tracking experiment with 30 participants (9 female, 21 male, mean age 24.6 years) who viewed 20 artisanal objects comprising basketry bags and ginger jars. Using a Pupil Labs eye-tracker, we recorded gaze patterns and generated heat maps representing human visual attention. Simultaneously, we analyzed the same objects using a pre-trained ViT model with DINO (Self-DIstillation with NO Labels), extracting attention maps from each of the 12 attention heads. We compared human and ViT attention distributions using Kullback-Leibler divergence across varying Gaussian parameters (sigma=0.1 to 3.0). Statistical analysis revealed optimal correlation at sigma=2.4 +-0.03, with attention head #12 showing the strongest alignment with human visual patterns. Significant differences were found between attention heads, with heads #7 and #9 demonstrating the greatest divergence from human attention (p< 0.05, Tukey HSD test). Results indicate that while ViTs exhibit more global attention patterns compared to human focal attention, certain attention heads can approximate human visual behavior, particularly for specific object features like buckles in basketry items. These findings suggest potential applications of ViT attention mechanisms in product design and aesthetic evaluation, while highlighting fundamental differences in attention strategies between human perception and current AI models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the alignment between human visual attention and the attention mechanisms of Vision Transformers (ViTs) when evaluating artisanal objects (baskets and ginger jars). An eye-tracking experiment was conducted to record gaze patterns of participants, while the same objects were analyzed using a pre-trained ViT model with DINO. The study compares human and ViT attention distributions using Kullback-Leibler divergence, finding an optimal correlation at a specific Gaussian parameter. Results suggest that while ViTs exhibit more global attention patterns, certain attention heads can approximate human visual behavior, particularly for specific object features. The paper explores potential applications of ViT attention in product design and aesthetic evaluation, while highlighting differences in attention strategies between humans and AI models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The idea of comparing ViT attention mechanisms to human visual attention in the context of *aesthetics* and *artisanal objects* has some degree of novelty.  While other studies have compared ViTs to human vision, the focus on aesthetic evaluation, specifically of handicrafts, is less common. This adds a specific domain focus not always explored.

**Significance:** The significance is more moderate.  The study identifies a correlation between specific heads in a ViT and human gaze patterns.  This *could* have practical implications for using ViTs to predict areas of interest in product design, but the presented work falls short of demonstrating this impact. The finding that ViTs demonstrate more global attention while humans focus on features like buckles is interesting, but requires more robust analysis to generalize. The conclusion that certain ViT components can approximate human attentional behavior, although stated, is not substantially different from what is observed in the vision model in general, therefore, no significant impact on the field is claimed.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clearly defined research question:** The paper clearly states its objective: to determine the correlation between ViT and human attention in a specific domain.
*   **Comprehensive methodology:** The study employs a well-defined methodology, including eye-tracking experiments, ViT analysis, and statistical comparisons.
*   **Detailed analysis:** The paper analyzes the performance of individual attention heads and the effect of varying Gaussian parameters.
*   **Visualizations:** The inclusion of heatmaps and boxplots helps to illustrate the findings.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited sample size:** A sample of 30 participants, with a skewed gender distribution, may limit the generalizability of the findings. More diverse samples and more participants could have given greater validity to the conclusions.
*   **Lack of behavioral validation:**  The study does not link visual attention to actual behavior (e.g., purchase decisions, ratings).  The findings would be strengthened by connecting attentional patterns to real-world actions.
*   **Limited interpretability:**  While the paper identifies correlations, it doesn't fully explain *why* certain ViT heads align better with human vision.  Further investigation into the features learned by these heads would be valuable.
*   **Moderate practical impact:** The practical implications for product design are only suggestive. The use of ViT for prediction in design and manufacturing will require additional testing to produce reliable models.

**Justification:**

The paper's novelty lies in the specific domain of aesthetic object evaluation and its approach to this topic through ViT comparison. However, its impact on the field is limited by its relatively small sample size and the lack of strong validation through behavioral analysis. More rigorous examination needs to be done, as the conclusions rely more on the observation of the human visual attention from eye tracking, whereas the actual ViT implementation and result on aesthetic and commercial results is missing. The interpretability and potential application of its findings need improvement.

### A Hybrid Early-Exit Algorithm for Large Language Models Based on Space Alignment Decoding (SPADE)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17618v1)
- **Authors**: Bowen Zheng, Ming Ma, Zhongqiao Lin, Tianming Yang
- **Abstract**: Large language models are computationally expensive due to their deep structures. Prior research has shown that intermediate layers contain sufficient information to generate accurate answers, leading to the development of early-exit algorithms that reduce inference costs by terminating computation at earlier layers. However, these methods often suffer from poor performance due to misalignment between intermediate and output layer representations that lead to decoding inaccuracy. To address these challenges, we propose SPADE (SPace Alignment DEcoding), a novel decoding method that aligns intermediate layer representations with the output layer by propagating a minimally reduced sequence consisting of only the start token and the answer token. We further optimize the early-exit decision-making process by training a linear approximation of SPADE that computes entropy-based confidence metrics. Putting them together, we create a hybrid early-exit algorithm that monitors confidence levels and stops inference at intermediate layers while using SPADE to generate high-quality outputs. This approach significantly reduces inference costs without compromising accuracy, offering a scalable and efficient solution for deploying large language models in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SPADE-EXIT, a hybrid early-exit algorithm for large language models (LLMs) designed to reduce inference costs without sacrificing accuracy. It addresses the problem of misalignment between intermediate layer representations and the output layer.  SPADE (SPace Alignment DEcoding) propagates a minimally reduced sequence (start token and answer token) through later layers to align representations.  L-SPADE, a linear approximation of SPADE, is used for fast confidence estimation to determine early-exit opportunities.  By monitoring confidence with L-SPADE and using SPADE for output generation at early exit points, the algorithm aims to achieve efficient inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces two key elements: SPADE and L-SPADE. The idea of using a minimal sequence (start token + answer token) for representation alignment is somewhat novel, although related to ideas of patching or probing the important states. L-SPADE as a linear approximation for faster confidence estimation is a natural extension of prior work on Tuned Lens, but its distillation target being SPADE is a nuanced improvement. The novelty lies in the *combination* of these ideas into a coherent early-exit strategy. The approach leverages the model's existing architecture in a unique way to decode intermediate representations. However, these aspects are incremental rather than a groundbreaking paradigm shift.
*   **Significance:** The significance of early-exit methods is clear: reducing the computational cost of LLM inference. SPADE-EXIT contributes to this effort by improving the accuracy of early-exit decisions and the quality of generated outputs at early layers. The results demonstrate improved performance over Logit Lens and Tuned Lens baselines, particularly in early layers. The experiments presented are thorough and demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach on multiple datasets and models. The exploration of generalization across datasets for L-SPADE training adds practical value. However, the overall impact might be limited because the approach is specific to decoder-only transformers and uses a small set of datasets for testing.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a practical problem: reducing LLM inference cost.
    *   Novel combination of SPADE and L-SPADE for efficient early exiting.
    *   Demonstrates improved accuracy and efficiency compared to existing methods.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation across multiple datasets and models.
    *   Analyzes the generalization capabilities of L-SPADE.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty of individual components is incremental.
    *   Limited testing across more diverse datasets and tasks (e.g., generation tasks beyond QA). The limitation section discusses this.
    *   The approach could be more broadly applicable, the paper only showcases experiments on decoder-only model.
    *   The code is not provided which limits reproducibility and further research.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's influence hinges on the practicality and adoption of SPADE-EXIT. If the algorithm proves effective in real-world applications and generalizes well to other tasks and architectures, it could impact the deployment and accessibility of LLMs. However, further research is needed to validate its robustness and scalability.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents its ideas and experimental results. The approach is technically sound, and the experiments are carefully designed. However, it is essential to acknowledge that the improvements achieved are incremental compared to prior work. The approach is not conceptually transformative and lacks widespread applications.

**Score: 6**

This score reflects the paper's solid contribution to the field of efficient LLM inference, acknowledging its incremental novelty, significance in improving existing early-exit methods, technical soundness, well-designed experiments, and a potential for practical impact while recognizing the limited novelty and the lack of general application.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Who Attacks, and Why? Using LLMs to Identify Negative Campaigning in 18M Tweets across 19 Countries
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17636v1)
- **Authors**: Victor Hartman, Petter Törnberg
- **Abstract**: Negative campaigning is a central feature of political competition, yet empirical research has been limited by the high cost and limited scalability of existing classification methods. This study makes two key contributions. First, it introduces zero-shot Large Language Models (LLMs) as a novel approach for cross-lingual classification of negative campaigning. Using benchmark datasets in ten languages, we demonstrate that LLMs achieve performance on par with native-speaking human coders and outperform conventional supervised machine learning approaches. Second, we leverage this novel method to conduct the largest cross-national study of negative campaigning to date, analyzing 18 million tweets posted by parliamentarians in 19 European countries between 2017 and 2022. The results reveal consistent cross-national patterns: governing parties are less likely to use negative messaging, while ideologically extreme and populist parties -- particularly those on the radical right -- engage in significantly higher levels of negativity. These findings advance our understanding of how party-level characteristics shape strategic communication in multiparty systems. More broadly, the study demonstrates the potential of LLMs to enable scalable, transparent, and replicable research in political communication across linguistic and cultural contexts.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Who Attacks, and Why? Using LLMs to Identify Negative Campaigning in 18M Tweets across 19 Countries".

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of negative campaigning by politicians across 19 European countries using a large dataset of 18 million tweets. It introduces a novel method of using zero-shot Large Language Models (LLMs) to classify negative campaigning in a cross-lingual context. The paper first demonstrates that LLMs can achieve performance comparable to human coders and superior to traditional supervised machine learning approaches on benchmark datasets in ten languages. It then leverages this method to analyze the large Twitter dataset, finding that governing parties are less likely to use negative messaging, while ideologically extreme and populist parties, especially those on the radical right, engage in significantly higher levels of negativity. The study highlights the potential of LLMs for scalable, transparent, and replicable research in political communication across linguistic and cultural contexts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Methodology:** The primary strength is the application of zero-shot LLMs to cross-lingual negative campaigning classification. While LLMs are increasingly used, this paper presents a valuable empirical demonstration of their effectiveness and efficiency in this specific domain, addressing a significant methodological challenge in comparative political communication research. The validation against existing manually coded datasets strengthens this claim.
*   **Large-Scale Data:** The study utilizes a large, cross-national dataset of 18 million tweets, which is a substantial contribution to the field. This dataset enables a more comprehensive analysis of negative campaigning patterns than previously possible.
*   **Substantive Findings:** The findings regarding the relationship between party characteristics (governing experience, ideological extremism, populism) and negative campaigning are theoretically grounded and empirically supported. These findings contribute to our understanding of strategic communication in multiparty systems.
*   **Transparency and Replicability:** The paper emphasizes the transparency and replicability of the LLM-based approach, which is crucial for advancing scientific knowledge in the field.
*   **Comparative Analysis:** The cross-national scope allows for valuable comparative insights, moving beyond the US-centric focus that often dominates the literature.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Generalizability of LLM Performance:** While the LLM performance is validated in ten languages, the assumption that classification accuracy generalizes to the remaining unvalidated languages introduces a degree of uncertainty. The authors acknowledge this limitation, but it still warrants consideration.
*   **Data Limitations:** The Twitter data, while large, has inherent limitations. Not all politicians use Twitter, and the content may not be representative of all political communication.
*   **Temporal Mismatch:** The temporal mismatch between the Twitter data (2017-2022) and the CHES data (2019) for party characteristics is a potential source of error. While the authors argue that most CHES indicators are relatively stable, this assumption may not hold for all parties or countries.
*   **Oversimplification of Government Status:** The use of a single government status variable is somewhat limiting. The dynamics of coalition governments are complex, and a binary indicator may not fully capture the nuances of governing experience.
*   **Limited Consideration of Country-Specific Context:** While the study includes country fixed effects, it could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of country-specific factors that may influence negative campaigning, such as electoral systems, media landscapes, and political cultures.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel methodological approach using LLMs for cross-lingual content analysis of political communication. This is a significant step towards addressing the scalability and cost challenges associated with traditional methods. The findings contribute meaningfully to our understanding of the drivers of negative campaigning in multiparty systems, expanding beyond the US context.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a substantial contribution, but it is not without limitations. The methodological innovation and large-scale analysis are commendable, but the data limitations and assumptions warrant some caution. The significance lies in the demonstrated potential of LLMs to revolutionize comparative political communication research, opening new avenues for large-scale, cross-national analysis. However, further research is needed to address the identified limitations and refine the LLM-based approach.

Score: 7.5

### CNS-Bench: Benchmarking Image Classifier Robustness Under Continuous Nuisance Shifts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17651v1)
- **Authors**: Olaf Dünkel, Artur Jesslen, Jiahao Xie, Christian Theobalt, Christian Rupprecht, Adam Kortylewski
- **Abstract**: An important challenge when using computer vision models in the real world is to evaluate their performance in potential out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios. While simple synthetic corruptions are commonly applied to test OOD robustness, they often fail to capture nuisance shifts that occur in the real world. Recently, diffusion models have been applied to generate realistic images for benchmarking, but they are restricted to binary nuisance shifts. In this work, we introduce CNS-Bench, a Continuous Nuisance Shift Benchmark to quantify OOD robustness of image classifiers for continuous and realistic generative nuisance shifts. CNS-Bench allows generating a wide range of individual nuisance shifts in continuous severities by applying LoRA adapters to diffusion models. To address failure cases, we propose a filtering mechanism that outperforms previous methods, thereby enabling reliable benchmarking with generative models. With the proposed benchmark, we perform a large-scale study to evaluate the robustness of more than 40 classifiers under various nuisance shifts. Through carefully designed comparisons and analyses, we find that model rankings can change for varying shifts and shift scales, which cannot be captured when applying common binary shifts. Additionally, we show that evaluating the model performance on a continuous scale allows the identification of model failure points, providing a more nuanced understanding of model robustness. Project page including code and data: https://genintel.github.io/CNS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CNS-Bench, a new benchmark for evaluating the out-of-distribution (OOD) robustness of image classifiers. Unlike existing benchmarks that rely on simple corruptions or binary nuisance shifts, CNS-Bench uses LoRA adapters applied to diffusion models to generate realistic images with continuous nuisance shifts (e.g., varying degrees of snow, different artistic styles).  It also introduces a novel filtering mechanism to remove out-of-class samples from the generated data. The authors use CNS-Bench to evaluate the robustness of over 40 classifiers along various axes like architecture, model size, and pre-training paradigm, revealing that model rankings can change based on the type and scale of the shift, and that some architectures are inherently more robust than others. They emphasize the importance of evaluating robustness as a spectrum, allowing for a more nuanced understanding of model failure points.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the **combination of continuous nuisance shifts with realistic image generation**, achieved through LoRA-adapted diffusion models. While diffusion models have been used for benchmarking before, the continuous aspect is a significant advancement. The proposed filtering mechanism for generated images is a necessary and helpful contribution, especially for scaling up benchmarking efforts. The large-scale empirical study across numerous models is also valuable.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a critical gap in existing OOD robustness evaluations. The authors clearly demonstrate that simple corruptions or binary shifts are insufficient for capturing the complexity of real-world distribution shifts. CNS-Bench provides a more controlled and scalable way to assess model performance under realistic and granular nuisance conditions. The results of the benchmark expose vulnerabilities and trade-offs of commonly used architectures. It can definitely have a significant impact in the robustness evaluation community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Realistic Image Generation:**  Leveraging diffusion models with LoRA provides a significant improvement in image realism compared to traditional synthetic corruptions.
    *   **Continuous Nuisance Shifts:** Captures gradual changes in distribution shift severity, allowing for the identification of failure points.
    *   **Scalability:** The approach allows for the generation of a large number of diverse images, making large-scale benchmarking feasible.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The thorough evaluation across various architectures, model sizes, and pre-training methods provides valuable insights.
    *   **Filtering Mechanism:** The introduction of an effective image filtering mechanism for OOC examples is very important and can help the community.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Diffusion Model Biases:**  The generated images inherently inherit biases from the underlying diffusion model and its training data. This could limit the generalizability of the benchmark.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Training LoRA adapters and generating large datasets remains computationally expensive, potentially limiting accessibility for researchers with limited resources.
    *   **Lack of Fine Grained Evaluation of Test Sets** Although the fine-grained LoRA adaptations enable the evaluation on various shifts, the test sets are still built with single shifts and not by combinations of shift which may appear in many tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:**  CNS-Bench has the potential to become a widely used benchmark for evaluating OOD robustness, especially as diffusion models become more accessible. The emphasis on continuous shifts and failure point analysis could influence future research directions in robustness and generalization. This work will prompt the community to explore methods to improve generalizability and resilience to realistic perturbations beyond simplistic corruptions.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper provides a significant and novel contribution to the field of OOD robustness evaluation. The introduction of continuous nuisance shifts with realistic image generation fills a critical gap in existing benchmarks and provides a more comprehensive and scalable approach to assessing model performance. The paper's strengths, including the scalability, comprehensive evaluation, and the filtering mechanism are well-executed. The primary limitations stem from the inherent biases and computational costs associated with diffusion models. Despite these weaknesses, the paper's potential influence on the field is substantial, warranting a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Attention (as Discrete-Time Markov) Chains
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17657v1)
- **Authors**: Yotam Erel, Olaf Dünkel, Rishabh Dabral, Vladislav Golyanik, Christian Theobalt, Amit H. Bermano
- **Abstract**: We introduce a new interpretation of the attention matrix as a discrete-time Markov chain. Our interpretation sheds light on common operations involving attention scores such as selection, summation, and averaging in a unified framework. It further extends them by considering indirect attention, propagated through the Markov chain, as opposed to previous studies that only model immediate effects. Our main observation is that tokens corresponding to semantically similar regions form a set of metastable states, where the attention clusters, while noisy attention scores tend to disperse. Metastable states and their prevalence can be easily computed through simple matrix multiplication and eigenanalysis, respectively. Using these lightweight tools, we demonstrate state-of-the-art zero-shot segmentation. Lastly, we define TokenRank -- the steady state vector of the Markov chain, which measures global token importance. We demonstrate that using it brings improvements in unconditional image generation. We believe our framework offers a fresh view of how tokens are being attended in modern visual transformers.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Attention (as Discrete-Time Markov) Chains":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel interpretation of the attention mechanism in transformers by framing it as a discrete-time Markov chain (DTMC). The attention matrix is viewed as a transition probability matrix between tokens (states). This DTMC perspective allows the authors to leverage tools from Markov chain theory to analyze and extend existing attention operations. Key contributions include:

1.  **DTMC Interpretation:**  Reframing attention matrices as DTMCs, providing a new lens for understanding attention operations (selection, summation, averaging).
2.  **Indirect Attention:** Modeling higher-order attention effects by considering multiple transitions (bounces) in the Markov chain, going beyond just direct token relationships.
3.  **Metastable States:**  Identifying tokens corresponding to semantically similar regions as metastable states within the Markov chain, allowing for clustering and noise reduction.
4.  **TokenRank:** Defining a "TokenRank" metric based on the steady-state vector of the DTMC, measuring the global importance of tokens within the sequence.
5.  **Applications:** Demonstrating the utility of the DTMC framework in zero-shot segmentation (achieving state-of-the-art results) and improving unconditional image generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of viewing the attention mechanism through a DTMC lens is, in itself, a novel contribution. It opens up a new perspective on analyzing and manipulating attention. The introduction of concepts like "metastable states" and "TokenRank" within this context builds upon this novelty. Furthermore, the multi-bounce concept to model higher order relationship between tokens is a compelling one.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from several factors:

    *   **Unified Framework:** The DTMC framework provides a unified way to understand various existing attention operations, streamlining their analysis.
    *   **Improved Performance:** The demonstrated improvements in zero-shot segmentation and image generation highlight the practical benefits of the proposed approach. Achieving state-of-the-art zero-shot segmentation is a notable accomplishment.
    *   **Interpretability:** The DTMC framework, especially TokenRank, contributes to improved interpretability of transformer models, offering insights into how tokens are related and which ones are most important. This is valuable given the ongoing efforts to understand and control these complex models.
    *   **Potential for Future Research:** The DTMC framing offers a rich area for future research, leveraging the extensive literature and tools available for Markov chains.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the DTMC interpretation and its implications.
    *   The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating the practical value of the proposed approach.
    *   The paper connects well to existing literature on attention mechanisms and Markov chains.
    *   The concept of modeling indirect attention effects is insightful and potentially widely applicable.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Complexity:** The iterative power method for calculating the steady-state vector may be computationally expensive for very long sequences. The paper could benefit from discussing optimization strategies to address this.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The PageRank formulation introduces a hyperparameter (alpha) for controlling teleportation probability. The paper could benefit from more in-depth discussion of how this parameter impacts performance and how to choose a suitable value. The sensitivity around alpha would also highlight the limitations of the proposed methodology.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides an intuitive explanation of the DTMC framework, a more rigorous theoretical analysis (e.g., convergence guarantees, error bounds) would strengthen the results.
    *   **Limited Exploration:** TokenRank with different number of hops or other variations of the metric has not been evaluated.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on attention mechanisms in transformers. The DTMC framework could inspire new approaches to analyzing, understanding, and manipulating attention, leading to improved performance and interpretability of these models. The TokenRank can serve as new metric for the evaluation of attention quality, given its strong experimental results.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper demonstrates clear benefits and presents compelling results, it is held back from a higher score due to the lack of theoretical results and hyperparameter choices. Furthermore, given the computational overhead associated with the proposed methodology, the improvements in performance are not drastic.

### See the Forest and the Trees: A Synergistic Reasoning Framework for Knowledge-Based Visual Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17659v1)
- **Authors**: Junjie Wang, Yunhan Tang, Yijie Wang, Zhihao Yuan, Huan Wang, Yangfan He, Bin Li
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have pushed the frontiers of Knowledge-Based Visual Question Answering (KBVQA), yet their reasoning is fundamentally bottlenecked by a reliance on uni-dimensional evidence. This "seeing only the trees, but not the forest" approach prevents robust, multi-faceted understanding. Inspired by the principle of seeing both the forest and trees, we propose Synergos-VQA, a novel synergistic reasoning framework. At its core, Synergos-VQA concurrently generates and fuses three complementary evidence streams at inference time: (1) Holistic Evidence to perceive the entire scene (the "forest"), (2) Structural Evidence from a prototype-driven module to identify key objects (the "trees"), and (3) Causal Evidence from a counterfactual probe to ensure the reasoning is robustly grounded. By synergistically fusing this multi-faceted evidence, our framework achieves a more comprehensive and reliable reasoning process. Extensive experiments show that Synergos-VQA decisively establishes a new state-of-the-art on three challenging benchmarks, including OK-VQA and A-OKVQA. Furthermore, our approach demonstrates strong plug-and-play capabilities, significantly boosting various open-source MLLMs and proving that superior methodological design can outperform sheer model scale.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Synergos-VQA, a novel framework for Knowledge-Based Visual Question Answering (KBVQA) designed to overcome the limitations of existing methods that rely on uni-dimensional evidence. Synergos-VQA synergistically fuses three complementary evidence streams at inference time: Holistic Evidence (perceiving the entire scene), Structural Evidence (identifying key objects), and Causal Evidence (ensuring robust grounding via counterfactual probing). This multi-faceted approach achieves state-of-the-art results on OK-VQA, A-OKVQA, and ScienceQA benchmarks.  The framework is built using open-source models and demonstrates plug-and-play capabilities, improving performance across diverse MLLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the synergistic fusion of three distinct evidence streams for KBVQA. While individual components (e.g., scene captioning, object detection, and counterfactual reasoning) have been explored previously in the context of VQA, the framework integrates them into a cohesive and self-contained system tailored to address the key limitations of uni-dimensional approaches. The "seeing both the forest and trees" analogy provides a compelling rationale for combining these perspectives. The prototype-driven CoT is also a genuinely new approach. The design, implementation, and specific application of the causal reasoning probe also offer a novel way to approach the problem.

**Significance:**  The paper demonstrates a clear improvement over existing state-of-the-art methods across several challenging KBVQA datasets. This suggests a genuine advance in the ability of models to reason about visual information using external knowledge. The implementation using open-source models and the demonstrated plug-and-play capability are significant because they lower the barrier to entry for researchers and practitioners. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component, which can guide future research in this area. The analysis of the failure cases helps to understand the limitations of the proposed model as well as possible directions for future work.  The speed analysis is also significant.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The paper provides convincing evidence of the effectiveness of Synergos-VQA, establishing a new state-of-the-art on multiple benchmarks.
*   **Clear and Well-Motivated Design:** The rationale for the synergistic approach is well-articulated, and the individual components are thoughtfully designed to address specific limitations.
*   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The thorough ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component and validate the synergistic effect.
*   **Reproducibility and Accessibility:**  The use of open-source models and the detailed implementation details make the framework more accessible to the research community.
*   **Failure Case Analysis:**  The inclusion of a failure case analysis demonstrates a commitment to understanding the limitations of the approach and provides directions for future research.
*   **Speed Analysis**: The speed analysis is thorough, explaining how the new method does not simply achieve a higher score, but also processes the data more efficiently.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The framework is complex, involving multiple modules and a carefully orchestrated pipeline. While the paper argues for the benefits of this complexity, it may be a barrier to adoption for some researchers.
*   **Reliance on Component Models:** The performance of Synergos-VQA depends on the performance of the individual component models (e.g., DETR, Qwen2.5-VL-7B, T5). Improvements in these component models could potentially further improve the overall performance, but also highlight the reliance on external progress.
*   **Limited Scope of Prototype Library:** As noted in the failure case analysis, the prototype library may lack precise prototypes for specialized objects, limiting the framework's generalization ability in certain domains.
*   **While the analysis on the hyperparameter k is compelling, it also reveals that this number may be dataset dependent.** This is not the largest issue, but it means that more effort will have to be put into model tuning for other datasets.

**Overall:**

This is a strong paper that presents a novel and effective framework for KBVQA. The synergistic approach, strong empirical results, and detailed analysis make it a significant contribution to the field. The use of open-source models and the plug-and-play capability increase the accessibility and potential impact of the work. While the complexity of the framework and the reliance on component models are potential limitations, the benefits outweigh the drawbacks.

### Simulating multiple human perspectives in socio-ecological systems using large language models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17680v1)
- **Authors**: Yongchao Zeng, Calum Brown, Ioannis Kyriakou, Ronja Hotz, Mark Rounsevell
- **Abstract**: Understanding socio-ecological systems requires insights from diverse stakeholder perspectives, which are often hard to access. To enable alternative, simulation-based exploration of different stakeholder perspectives, we develop the HoPeS (Human-Oriented Perspective Shifting) modelling framework. HoPeS employs agents powered by large language models (LLMs) to represent various stakeholders; users can step into the agent roles to experience perspectival differences. A simulation protocol serves as a "scaffold" to streamline multiple perspective-taking simulations, supporting users in reflecting on, transitioning between, and integrating across perspectives. A prototype system is developed to demonstrate HoPeS in the context of institutional dynamics and land use change, enabling both narrative-driven and numerical experiments. In an illustrative experiment, a user successively adopts the perspectives of a system observer and a researcher - a role that analyses data from the embedded land use model to inform evidence-based decision-making for other LLM agents representing various institutions. Despite the user's effort to recommend technically sound policies, discrepancies persist between the policy recommendation and implementation due to stakeholders' competing advocacies, mirroring real-world misalignment between researcher and policymaker perspectives. The user's reflection highlights the subjective feelings of frustration and disappointment as a researcher, especially due to the challenge of maintaining political neutrality while attempting to gain political influence. Despite this, the user exhibits high motivation to experiment with alternative narrative framing strategies, suggesting the system's potential in exploring different perspectives. Further system and protocol refinement are likely to enable new forms of interdisciplinary collaboration in socio-ecological simulations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HoPeS (Human-Oriented Perspective Shifting), a framework that utilizes Large Language Models (LLMs) to simulate multiple human perspectives within socio-ecological systems. The core idea is to allow users to step into the roles of different stakeholders (represented by LLM-powered agents) within a simulation to understand the system dynamics from various viewpoints.  A simulation protocol acts as a scaffold, guiding the user through perspective-taking simulations and supporting reflection and integration of insights. A prototype system, InsNet-CRAFTY, is built to demonstrate HoPeS in the context of land use governance. A user experiment involves taking on the roles of system observer and research supplier, revealing challenges in policy communication and demonstrating the potential of the system for both narrative-driven and numerical experimentation. The authors reflect on the potential and limitations of the approach, including challenges related to LLM hallucination, role alignment, and the integration of diverse perspectives.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to enable perspective-shifting in socio-ecological simulations is innovative.  While agent-based modeling and participatory simulations exist, HoPeS represents a unique hybrid approach that leverages the linguistic capabilities of LLMs to create more immersive and nuanced social interactions. The framework's scaffolding protocol for guiding perspective taking is also a notable contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in socio-ecological modeling: the difficulty of incorporating diverse stakeholder perspectives effectively. By enabling users to experience the system from different viewpoints, HoPeS has the potential to promote deeper understanding of system dynamics, ethical dilemmas, and the impact of individual decisions.  This could be valuable for policy design, conflict resolution, and promoting more inclusive decision-making processes. The prototype system is a step in this direction.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear articulation of the framework:** The HoPeS framework is well-defined and explained.
    *   **Practical demonstration:** The prototype system and user experiment provide concrete examples of how HoPeS can be implemented and used.
    *   **Thorough discussion of limitations:** The authors acknowledge the challenges related to LLM hallucination, role alignment, and the evaluation of perspective-shifting effectiveness.
    *   **Interdisciplinary approach:** The paper bridges concepts from social psychology, cognitive science, and computational modeling.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited experimental validation:** The user experiment is illustrative but lacks a more rigorous evaluation of HoPeS's impact on user understanding and decision-making.  A larger study with multiple users and controlled comparisons is needed.
    *   **LLM limitations:** The paper acknowledges LLM hallucination, but the potential biases embedded in the LLMs used and their influence on the simulation results require more scrutiny. The alignment problem, ensuring LLMs accurately represent specific roles without harmful biases, is also significant.
    *   **Simplification of reality:**  While HoPeS offers increased realism compared to traditional ABMs, the model still simplifies the complex social and ecological interactions.  The influence of factors like power dynamics, social norms, and cultural contexts is not fully explored.
    *   **Lack of standardized evaluation metrics:** Defining clear quantitative metrics for assessing the effectiveness of perspective shifting, and for measuring the utility of resulting simulations, remains a challenge.

*   **Overall:** While the core concept and approach are novel, the paper's significance is currently limited by the early stage of development of the HoPeS framework, the lack of extensive validation, and remaining limitations of LLMs. More rigorous experiments and addressing inherent challenges in LLM use is needed to realize the framework's full potential.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**
The paper introduces a promising concept and demonstrates its feasibility with a functional prototype. The core strength lies in addressing a significant issue in socio-ecological modeling by incorporating a novel, hybrid approach involving LLMs and guided perspective-taking. However, the limited experimental validation and the inherent challenges associated with LLM use and data analysis prevent a higher score. Future studies should focus on addressing these challenges through more rigorous experimental designs to better quantify the effectiveness of HoPeS in enhancing system understanding and decision-making. Addressing concerns of potential biases embedded in pre-trained LLMs, and exploring techniques for aligning LLMs with specific roles is also important for the further development of HoPeS framework.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Generalized Dual Discriminator GANs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17684v1)
- **Authors**: Penukonda Naga Chandana, Tejas Srivastava, Gowtham R. Kurri, V. Lalitha
- **Abstract**: Dual discriminator generative adversarial networks (D2 GANs) were introduced to mitigate the problem of mode collapse in generative adversarial networks. In D2 GANs, two discriminators are employed alongside a generator: one discriminator rewards high scores for samples from the true data distribution, while the other favors samples from the generator. In this work, we first introduce dual discriminator $\alpha$-GANs (D2 $\alpha$-GANs), which combines the strengths of dual discriminators with the flexibility of a tunable loss function, $\alpha$-loss. We further generalize this approach to arbitrary functions defined on positive reals, leading to a broader class of models we refer to as generalized dual discriminator generative adversarial networks. For each of these proposed models, we provide theoretical analysis and show that the associated min-max optimization reduces to the minimization of a linear combination of an $f$-divergence and a reverse $f$-divergence. This generalizes the known simplification for D2-GANs, where the objective reduces to a linear combination of the KL-divergence and the reverse KL-divergence. Finally, we perform experiments on 2D synthetic data and use multiple performance metrics to capture various advantages of our GANs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a generalization of Dual Discriminator Generative Adversarial Networks (D2 GANs), called dual discriminator alpha-GANs (D2 a-GANs). D2 GANs, as a reminder, use two discriminators: one favoring real data and the other favoring generated data.  The key contribution is replacing the binary cross-entropy loss typically used in D2 GANs with the alpha-loss, a tunable loss function that interpolates between exponential loss, binary cross-entropy, and a soft 0-1 loss. Further, the paper extends this framework to arbitrary functions defined on positive reals, resulting in "generalized dual discriminator GANs." The paper provides theoretical analysis demonstrating that this framework reduces to minimizing a linear combination of f-divergences and reverse f-divergences. Finally, the paper presents experiments on synthetic 2D data showcasing improved performance compared to vanilla GANs and D2 GANs using metrics like symmetric KL divergence and Wasserstein distance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has moderate novelty.
    *   While D2 GANs and alpha-loss based GANs are known techniques, combining these two and generalizing them is novel. The theoretical analysis that shows the reduction to minimizing a combination of f-divergence and reverse f-divergence is a valuable contribution.
    *   The generalization to arbitrary functions defined on positive reals is also a significant contribution and gives a unified approach to the dual discriminator generative models.

*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate to high within the GAN research community.
    *   The D2 a-GANs and generalized D2 GANs offers a flexible framework that allows for better tuning of the generative models, which can address the mode collapse problem and improve the stability of training.
    *   Empirical results, though limited to 2D synthetic data, are promising. Demonstrating advantages on higher-dimensional datasets (e.g., images) would significantly enhance the paper's impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretical foundation:** The theoretical analysis is rigorous and provides valuable insights into the behavior of the proposed models. The generalization to arbitrary functions makes the analysis broader and potentially more useful.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is relatively well-written and structured, making it understandable.
    *   **Unified framework:** The D2 a-GANs unifies different loss functions.
    *   **Empirical evidence:** Results are promising.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited empirical evaluation:**  The experiments are conducted solely on synthetic 2D data. The lack of results on real-world, higher-dimensional datasets (like images) weakens the paper's practical significance. While the 2D synthetic data is a good starting point, GANs are primarily used on more complex, high-dimensional data. This is the paper's biggest weakness.
    *   **Incremental improvement:** The improvement over D2 GANs on synthetic data, while present, needs to be substantial to justify the increased complexity. It's unclear if the advantages hold up in practice on more realistic datasets.
    *   **Hyperparameter sensitivity:** GANs are notoriously sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. While the paper mentions hyperparameter tuning, it does not sufficiently address the sensitivity of the proposed models to different hyperparameter choices. This could be a practical impediment to their widespread adoption. The paper does not adequately convey guidance on how to optimally tune the parameters *a1*, *a2*, *c1*, and *c2*.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could potentially influence GAN research by providing a more flexible and robust framework for generative modeling. The theoretical analysis may guide the design of new GAN architectures and loss functions.
    *   The paper needs to be further evaluated. It would benefit the GANs research community to evaluate the method on benchmark datasets like CIFAR-10 or CelebA.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper's novelty lies in combining known concepts in a non-trivial manner and providing a generalized framework for dual discriminator GANs.  The theoretical analysis is a strong point. However, the lack of empirical validation on real-world datasets significantly limits its practical impact. While the synthetic data experiments show promise, they are insufficient to demonstrate the true potential of the proposed models. The improvement shown is not dramatically superior to existing approaches in the benchmark datasets of GANs. The hyperparameter sensitivity is also a concern. Without more robust empirical evidence, the paper's influence will be limited.

### Towards Greater Leverage: Scaling Laws for Efficient Mixture-of-Experts Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17702v2)
- **Authors**: Changxin Tian, Kunlong Chen, Jia Liu, Ziqi Liu, Zhiqiang Zhang, Jun Zhou
- **Abstract**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) has become a dominant architecture for scaling Large Language Models (LLMs) efficiently by decoupling total parameters from computational cost. However, this decoupling creates a critical challenge: predicting the model capacity of a given MoE configurations (e.g., expert activation ratio and granularity) remains an unresolved problem. To address this gap, we introduce Efficiency Leverage (EL), a metric quantifying the computational advantage of an MoE model over a dense equivalent. We conduct a large-scale empirical study, training over 300 models up to 28B parameters, to systematically investigate the relationship between MoE architectural configurations and EL. Our findings reveal that EL is primarily driven by the expert activation ratio and the total compute budget, both following predictable power laws, while expert granularity acts as a non-linear modulator with a clear optimal range. We integrate these discoveries into a unified scaling law that accurately predicts the EL of an MoE architecture based on its configuration. To validate our derived scaling laws, we designed and trained Ling-mini-beta, a pilot model for Ling-2.0 series with only 0.85B active parameters, alongside a 6.1B dense model for comparison. When trained on an identical 1T high-quality token dataset, Ling-mini-beta matched the performance of the 6.1B dense model while consuming over 7x fewer computational resources, thereby confirming the accuracy of our scaling laws. This work provides a principled and empirically-grounded foundation for the scaling of efficient MoE models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Towards Greater Leverage: Scaling Laws for Efficient Mixture-of-Experts Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a metric called "Efficiency Leverage" (EL) to quantify the computational advantage of Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models over their dense counterparts. The authors conduct a large-scale empirical study, training over 300 models up to 28B parameters, to investigate the relationship between MoE architectural configurations (expert activation ratio, granularity, etc.) and EL. They find that EL is primarily driven by the expert activation ratio and total compute budget, with expert granularity acting as a non-linear modulator. They integrate these findings into a unified scaling law to predict the EL of an MoE architecture based on its configuration. Finally, they validate their scaling laws by designing and training a pilot MoE model ("Ling-mini-beta") that achieves comparable performance to a larger dense model with significantly fewer computational resources.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty of the EL Metric:** The EL metric provides a simple and useful way to analyze the computational benefits of sparse MoE models.
*   **Large-Scale Empirical Study:** The extensive experiments, involving training hundreds of models, provide strong empirical support for their findings.
*   **Systematic Analysis of MoE Architectures:** The paper offers a systematic deconstruction of MoE architectures and analyzes the influence of various components (activation ratio, granularity, shared experts, etc.) on efficiency.
*   **Unified Scaling Law:** The integration of empirical findings into a unified scaling law is valuable for predicting the EL of MoE models and guiding efficient architecture design.
*   **Validation with Ling-mini-beta:** The successful design and training of Ling-mini-beta serves as a practical validation of the derived scaling laws.
*   **Clarity of Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains complex concepts.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Independence Assumption:** The paper assumes that the effects of different MoE architectural factors are largely independent. While this assumption simplifies the analysis, it may overlook important interaction effects between these factors.
*   **FLOPs as the Sole Cost Metric:** The analysis focuses solely on FLOPs as the measure of computational cost, neglecting other important factors such as memory bandwidth, communication overhead, and hardware-specific optimizations. Therefore, it's largely a hardware-agnostic analysis which may not translate into specific speedups in real-world implementations without further optimization.
*   **Limited Scope of Validation:**  The validation of the scaling law is primarily based on the Ling-mini-beta experiment. While successful, further validation on different datasets and model scales would strengthen the generalizability of their findings.
*   **Focus on Loss:** While the paper acknowledges that model performance can be quantified using various methods, the validation process is primarily based on minimizing loss value only. A focus on benchmark scores is also critical to holistically validate scaling laws.
*   **Limited novel exploration:** The scaling laws for MoE models have been widely explored before, and this paper touches on different parameters that may affect it, but it still only offers an incremental improvement.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by providing a principled and empirically-grounded foundation for scaling efficient MoE models. The EL metric and derived scaling laws offer valuable tools for researchers and practitioners to design and optimize MoE architectures for large-scale language modeling. The validation with Ling-mini-beta demonstrates the potential for achieving significant efficiency gains with carefully designed MoE models.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper's findings are likely to influence future research on MoE models, guiding the development of more efficient and scalable architectures. The EL metric could become a standard benchmark for evaluating the computational advantage of MoE models. The unified scaling law may be incorporated into automated model design and optimization tools.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **7** out of 10.

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution to the MoE scaling law literature by systematically exploring the impact of architectural configurations on computational efficiency. The EL metric is a simple but effective tool for comparing different MoE models, and the large-scale empirical study provides strong support for the authors' findings. The unification of these findings into a unified scaling law, validated through the Ling-mini-beta experiment, demonstrates the practical utility of their approach.

However, the limitations related to the independence assumption, the reliance on FLOPs as the sole cost metric, the limited scope of validation, and the lack of attention to memory constraints and hardware aspects hold the paper back. Addressing these limitations in future work could further increase the impact and generalizability of their findings. Given the paper builds incrementally on earlier work in MoE scaling, the novelty is considered significant, but not revolutionary.
