    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The paper combines existing techniques (LoRA, GRPO, quantization). While the synthesis is valuable, the individual components are well-established, thus, diminishing the novelty claim.
    *   **Limited Benchmarking:** While the authors state that GRPO increases performance over PPO and other LLM training methods, a benchmark comparison of these methods would have strengthened the paper.

*   **Novelty:** The combination of GRPO with parameter-efficient techniques (LoRA) on quantized models is a useful contribution. The empirical scaling laws, while limited by dataset specificity, offer a practical framework for understanding training dynamics.
*   **Significance:** Improving the efficiency of LLM training has broad implications. If the scaling laws can be generalized, the work has the potential to significantly reduce the computational burden of fine-tuning reasoning models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a contribution towards a crucial problem (efficient LLM fine-tuning). However, the dataset specificity, model size limitations, and reliance on existing techniques limit its novelty and broader impact. The empirical scaling laws are valuable but require validation across a wider range of conditions to demonstrate their true generalizability. The parameter-efficient fine-tuning approach is a strength. Although the results do not apply to all model training setups or LLM models, the authors have been transparent regarding the parameters that they used. For these reasons, a score of 6 reflects a fair evaluation of the paper's strengths and weaknesses.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### NeuralDB: Scaling Knowledge Editing in LLMs to 100,000 Facts with Neural KV Database
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18028v1)
- **Authors**: Weizhi Fei, Hao Shi, Jing Xu, Jingchen Peng, Jiazheng Li, Jingzhao Zhang, Bo Bai, Wei Han, Zhenyuan Chen, Xueyan Niu
- **Abstract**: Efficiently editing knowledge stored in large language models (LLMs) enables model updates without large-scale training. One possible solution is Locate-and-Edit (L\&E), allowing simultaneous modifications of a massive number of facts. However, such editing may compromise the general abilities of LLMs and even result in forgetting edited facts when scaling up to thousands of edits. In this paper, we model existing linear L\&E methods as querying a Key-Value (KV) database. From this perspective, we then propose NeuralDB, an editing framework that explicitly represents the edited facts as a neural KV database equipped with a non-linear gated retrieval module, % In particular, our gated module only operates when inference involves the edited facts, effectively preserving the general abilities of LLMs. Comprehensive experiments involving the editing of 10,000 facts were conducted on the ZsRE and CounterFacts datasets, using GPT2-XL, GPT-J (6B) and Llama-3 (8B). The results demonstrate that NeuralDB not only excels in editing efficacy, generalization, specificity, fluency, and consistency, but also preserves overall performance across six representative text understanding and generation tasks. Further experiments indicate that NeuralDB maintains its effectiveness even when scaled to 100,000 facts (\textbf{50x} more than in prior work).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NeuralDB, a new framework for scaling knowledge editing (KE) in large language models (LLMs). It proposes representing edited facts as a neural Key-Value (KV) database integrated into the LLM's feedforward network (FFN) layer using a gated retrieval module.  This approach aims to overcome limitations of existing locate-and-edit (L&E) methods, particularly their performance degradation when scaling to thousands of edits and their reliance on sampling from Wikipedia to preserve general knowledge. The paper claims NeuralDB excels in editing efficacy, generalization, specificity, fluency, and consistency, while preserving overall performance across various text understanding and generation tasks. Experimental results on GPT2-XL, GPT-J, and Llama-3 models, involving editing up to 100,000 facts, are presented to support these claims.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The idea of representing knowledge edits as a neural KV store is interesting. The paper builds on the locate-and-edit paradigm, reframing existing linear L&E methods as querying a KV database. Introducing a *non-linear* gated retrieval mechanism to access this database appears to be the main novelty, allowing for more precise retrieval of edited information and preventing interference with general knowledge. However, the general idea of using a KV store for knowledge is not entirely novel, and some prior work uses external modules with caches for performing a similar task. The contribution is more in how the KV store is integrated *within* the LLM and the specific gating mechanism employed.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its ability to scale knowledge editing to significantly larger numbers of facts (100,000) compared to previous methods (hundreds/thousands).  The paper suggests that the proposed method maintains higher performance and general abilities compared to existing L&E techniques as the number of edits increases. If true, this is an important step towards more adaptable and trustworthy LLMs. The experiments and evaluations covering several models and datasets are comprehensive, strengthening the paper's claims. The idea of having a plug-and-play module makes updates, deletions, and modifications flexible.

**Strengths:**

*   **Scalability:**  The most significant strength is the claimed ability to scale knowledge editing to 100,000 facts. This considerably exceeds the capacity of previous methods.
*   **Preservation of General Abilities:** The paper presents evidence that NeuralDB is effective at preserving the general capabilities of LLMs, a crucial aspect that other methods often struggle with.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper provides extensive experimental results, evaluating the framework across various models, datasets, and metrics.
*   **Interpretability and Flexibility:**  The gated mechanism and the explicit KV database offer greater interpretability and flexibility in managing edited facts.
* **Comparisons with Previous Methods:** The experiments involve comparisons with state-of-the-art methods for knowledge editing.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited comparison to Memory-based methods:** The comparison to methods with an external module cache is missing. It's imperative to compare against these methods in order to evaluate the merits of integrating the KV database directly in the FFN layer, instead of relying on external retrieval mechanisms.
*   **Incremental editing in general:** In section 4 the method is claimed to be "ease of deployment" and facilitates "addition, deletion, and modification." However, the experiments use large scale batch editing. It is not entirely clear from the paper if the proposed method is any easier to maintain than the baseline locate-and-edit methods (requiring recomputation of the A updates).
*   **Lack of Ablation on Gating Function:** The ablation study on the gating function parameters is limited. A deeper understanding of the gating function and how it relates to overall performance should be provided.
*   **Memory Overhead:** The paper mentions an increasing memory usage. Although the percentage relative to the model size may seem small, it could be a bottleneck for very large models or resource-constrained environments. Further analysis of the trade-off between performance and memory usage is needed.
*  **Potential Overfitting:** The proposed framework allows for very high precision of edited facts but there is a possibility that the framework is overfitting for a very high precision of the edited fact at the cost of overall language understanding.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**.

*   The paper addresses a significant challenge in the field of knowledge editing: scaling the number of edits while preserving model performance. The proposed approach, NeuralDB, presents a novel integration of a KV database with a non-linear gating mechanism to address this challenge.
* The significance is clearly showcased in the amount of edited facts that the model can handle, compared to previous methods.
* The experimental results are comprehensive, across multiple models and datasets, but missing comparison to memory-based models and the effect of incremental edits.
* There is still a certain amount of memory overhead in scaling and it remains to be seen how that tradeoff will be perceived.
*   Further, more detailed ablation studies could strengthen the paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### ViGText: Deepfake Image Detection with Vision-Language Model Explanations and Graph Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18031v1)
- **Authors**: Ahmad ALBarqawi, Mahmoud Nazzal, Issa Khalil, Abdallah Khreishah, NhatHai Phan
- **Abstract**: The rapid rise of deepfake technology, which produces realistic but fraudulent digital content, threatens the authenticity of media. Traditional deepfake detection approaches often struggle with sophisticated, customized deepfakes, especially in terms of generalization and robustness against malicious attacks. This paper introduces ViGText, a novel approach that integrates images with Vision Large Language Model (VLLM) Text explanations within a Graph-based framework to improve deepfake detection. The novelty of ViGText lies in its integration of detailed explanations with visual data, as it provides a more context-aware analysis than captions, which often lack specificity and fail to reveal subtle inconsistencies. ViGText systematically divides images into patches, constructs image and text graphs, and integrates them for analysis using Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) to identify deepfakes. Through the use of multi-level feature extraction across spatial and frequency domains, ViGText captures details that enhance its robustness and accuracy to detect sophisticated deepfakes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ViGText significantly enhances generalization and achieves a notable performance boost when it detects user-customized deepfakes. Specifically, average F1 scores rise from 72.45% to 98.32% under generalization evaluation, and reflects the model's superior ability to generalize to unseen, fine-tuned variations of stable diffusion models. As for robustness, ViGText achieves an increase of 11.1% in recall compared to other deepfake detection approaches. When facing targeted attacks that exploit its graph-based architecture, ViGText limits classification performance degradation to less than 4%. ViGText uses detailed visual and textual analysis to set a new standard for detecting deepfakes, helping ensure media authenticity and information integrity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ViGText, a novel approach for deepfake image detection. ViGText integrates visual information with text explanations generated by a Vision Large Language Model (VLLM) within a graph-based framework. The image is divided into patches, and both image and text graphs are constructed, incorporating spatial and frequency domain features. The graphs are then analyzed using Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) to identify deepfakes. The key novelty lies in the integration of detailed explanations (going beyond simple image captions) with visual data in a structured graph, enabling the model to capture subtle inconsistencies. The paper demonstrates improved generalization to unseen, fine-tuned generative models and robustness against adversarial attacks, particularly those exploiting foundation models. Experiments show significant performance boosts, especially in F1 scores, compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach by effectively combining VLLM generated explanations with visual features. The use of graphs to represent and integrate these modalities is unique and goes beyond simple concatenation or attention mechanisms. While prior works have explored textual information for deepfake detection, this study introduces a new level of granularity and structured integration of that information.

*   **Significance:** The problem of detecting deepfakes, particularly those generated by sophisticated and user-customized models, is highly significant. The improvements in generalization and robustness claimed by the authors address critical weaknesses in existing methods. The results presented suggest that the method has the potential to become a standard in the field for assessing these types of attacks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method provides a robust framework by integrating visual and textual information, going beyond traditional captioning approaches.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate improvements in both generalization and robustness across various datasets and attack scenarios.
    *   The analysis of sensitivity to design choices (e.g., patch size) is insightful.
    *   The authors release their data and code, promoting reproducibility and future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The threat model, while covering various adversarial settings, is primarily evaluated on fully synthetic images. The paper acknowledges a limitation of the model's capabilities on "real-world partial manipulations such as face swaps, reenactments, or spliced content." This poses a challenge for deploying the model in practice.
    *   The framework currently only supports images, and the extension of the framework to the audio and video modalities would be a significant challenge.
    *   Although promising results for adversarial robustness are discussed, more analysis of the complexity of an attack that would work on the presented model is needed.
    *   The sensitivity study performed is rather limited. It would have been more useful to include a more extensive ablation study of the various components that influence the performance of ViGText.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper offers a practical solution to the challenge of detecting deepfakes using detailed explanations of the visual elements involved. It offers a unique approach to combine visual and text modalities that may inspire future detection research.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel approach for deepfake detection that addresses key limitations in existing methods. The use of VLLM-generated explanations integrated within a graph structure has significant potential to improve robustness against evolving generative models and adversarial attacks. The experimental evaluation is comprehensive and the performance gains are notable, particularly with regards to challenging new test scenarios. However, the limited real-world partial manipulation capabilities, as well as the absence of a more extensive ablation study, limits the scope of the results that are presented. Despite these shortcomings, the concept is novel and promising.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### OpenNav: Open-World Navigation with Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18033v1)
- **Authors**: Mingfeng Yuan, Letian Wang, Steven L. Waslander
- **Abstract**: Pre-trained large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong common-sense reasoning abilities, making them promising for robotic navigation and planning tasks. However, despite recent progress, bridging the gap between language descriptions and actual robot actions in the open-world, beyond merely invoking limited predefined motion primitives, remains an open challenge. In this work, we aim to enable robots to interpret and decompose complex language instructions, ultimately synthesizing a sequence of trajectory points to complete diverse navigation tasks given open-set instructions and open-set objects. We observe that multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) exhibit strong cross-modal understanding when processing free-form language instructions, demonstrating robust scene comprehension. More importantly, leveraging their code-generation capability, MLLMs can interact with vision-language perception models to generate compositional 2D bird-eye-view value maps, effectively integrating semantic knowledge from MLLMs with spatial information from maps to reinforce the robot's spatial understanding. To further validate our approach, we effectively leverage large-scale autonomous vehicle datasets (AVDs) to validate our proposed zero-shot vision-language navigation framework in outdoor navigation tasks, demonstrating its capability to execute a diverse range of free-form natural language navigation instructions while maintaining robustness against object detection errors and linguistic ambiguities. Furthermore, we validate our system on a Husky robot in both indoor and outdoor scenes, demonstrating its real-world robustness and applicability. Supplementary videos are available at https://trailab.github.io/OpenNav-website/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "OpenNav" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OpenNav, a novel zero-shot vision-language navigation (VLN) framework that enables robots to navigate in open-world environments using free-form language instructions. OpenNav leverages a multi-modal large language model (MLLM) coupled with an open-vocabulary perception system (OVPS) to interpret instructions, understand the scene, and generate trajectories. The MLLM generates code to interact with the OVPS, creating a bird's-eye-view (BEV) value map that integrates semantic knowledge with spatial information. A trajectory optimizer refines the generated trajectory for collision avoidance and geometric compliance. The system is validated using autonomous vehicle datasets (AVDs) and a real-world robot, demonstrating its robustness to object detection errors and linguistic ambiguities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper claims several novel contributions. The primary novelty lies in the zero-shot approach, eliminating the need for in-context examples, pre-trained skills, or motion primitives for outdoor VLN tasks with open-set objects and instructions. The multi-expert system integrating MLLMs and OVPS is also presented as a novel way to enhance scene comprehension, as opposed to relying solely on object detectors.  The use of a task-agnostic prompt is another claim to improve generalization.

However, several components build upon existing work. MLLMs are increasingly used in robotics, and the concept of using them for high-level planning isn't entirely new. Similarly, OVPSs that combine object detection, segmentation, and captioning exist in the literature. A potential weakness lies in the incremental nature of the approach; it leverages existing models (e.g., ChatGPT-4o, Grounding DINO, RAM, TAP) without substantial modifications. The code generation approach for trajectory synthesis is interesting, but the details of the code generation process are not fully elaborated, potentially limiting its reproducibility or impact.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a significant challenge in robotics: enabling robots to operate in unstructured, open-world environments with diverse instructions. The reported experimental results, particularly the improvement in ambiguous object navigation and high-level instruction following compared to the LLM-TG baseline, suggest practical value. The ablation study provides some insights into the contributions of each component.

A significant weakness is the reliance on simulated environments using existing AVDs, which might not fully capture the complexities and uncertainties of real-world deployments. While the paper mentions real-world robot experiments, the limited details and lack of quantitative analysis raise concerns about the system's robustness and scalability in real-world settings.

The paper also lacks a strong comparison with other recent zero-shot VLN methods. The choice of LLM-TG as the baseline is not entirely justified.  A comparison with other state-of-the-art approaches, even if those methods are not strictly zero-shot, would have provided a better context for evaluating the performance of OpenNav.

**Score Justification:**

Given the above analysis, a score of **6** is appropriate. While the paper demonstrates a potentially useful system and addresses a significant problem in robotics, the novelty is somewhat incremental, building on existing models and techniques. The lack of a strong comparative analysis and limited real-world validation weaken the paper's impact. The work needs more rigorous evaluations in real-world settings and a comparison with more relevant state-of-the-art to truly demonstrate its significance. While the integration of different components is interesting, the level of contribution to each component is limited.
Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 6/10

### Removing Box-Free Watermarks for Image-to-Image Models via Query-Based Reverse Engineering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18034v1)
- **Authors**: Haonan An, Guang Hua, Hangcheng Cao, Zhengru Fang, Guowen Xu, Susanto Rahardja, Yuguang Fang
- **Abstract**: The intellectual property of deep generative networks (GNets) can be protected using a cascaded hiding network (HNet) which embeds watermarks (or marks) into GNet outputs, known as box-free watermarking. Although both GNet and HNet are encapsulated in a black box (called operation network, or ONet), with only the generated and marked outputs from HNet being released to end users and deemed secure, in this paper, we reveal an overlooked vulnerability in such systems. Specifically, we show that the hidden GNet outputs can still be reliably estimated via query-based reverse engineering, leaking the generated and unmarked images, despite the attacker's limited knowledge of the system. Our first attempt is to reverse-engineer an inverse model for HNet under the stringent black-box condition, for which we propose to exploit the query process with specially curated input images. While effective, this method yields unsatisfactory image quality. To improve this, we subsequently propose an alternative method leveraging the equivalent additive property of box-free model watermarking and reverse-engineering a forward surrogate model of HNet, with better image quality preservation. Extensive experimental results on image processing and image generation tasks demonstrate that both attacks achieve impressive watermark removal success rates (100%) while also maintaining excellent image quality (reaching the highest PSNR of 34.69 dB), substantially outperforming existing attacks, highlighting the urgent need for robust defensive strategies to mitigate the identified vulnerability in box-free model watermarking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a vulnerability in box-free watermarking schemes for image-to-image models, which are designed to protect the intellectual property of deep generative networks (GNets). The authors demonstrate that even when the GNet and the watermarking network (HNet) are encapsulated as a black-box (ONet), an attacker can still reliably estimate the hidden outputs of the GNet by querying the ONet and reverse-engineering the HNet. They propose two query-based reverse engineering attacks: (1) training an inverse model of HNet and (2) exploiting the additive equivalence property of box-free model watermarking to estimate a forward surrogate model of HNet. The attacks effectively remove watermarks while maintaining good image quality. The authors conduct experiments on image processing and generation tasks, demonstrating the effectiveness of their attacks and highlighting the need for stronger defenses. Finally, they suggest a query screening mechanism to mitigate the proposed attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper reveals a non-trivial vulnerability in a seemingly secure watermarking scheme. The insight that query-based reverse engineering can be used to circumvent the black-box protection of GNet and HNet is novel. While reverse engineering attacks are not entirely new, the application and adaptation of such techniques to the specific context of box-free watermarking and the exploitation of the additive property are innovative.  The approaches are well-defined and the presentation is clearly articulated and technically sound.

*   **Significance:**  The work has considerable significance because it exposes a practical weakness in a watermarking approach intended to safeguard valuable deep learning models.  The finding that HNet's protection can be bypassed opens up a new attack vector and urges the development of more robust watermarking strategies.  By demonstrating the feasibility of the attacks under a realistic threat model, the paper underscores the limitations of current defense mechanisms. The suggestion of API detection as a countermeasure is a valuable addition, although it may not be a perfect solution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem and threat model.
    *   Clear explanation of the proposed attacks, including their underlying rationale.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of the attacks on image processing and generation tasks.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation against existing defenses.
    *   The detailed discussion and analysis of the additive property of the watermark is well done and contributes to the robustness of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the API detection mechanism is mentioned, the paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of its limitations and potential for evasion.  Specifically, is it possible to craft adversarial examples that bypass the API detection while still enabling watermark removal?
    *   The attacks rely on a specific assumption (i.e., being able to bypass GNet via appropriate queries). The applicability of the attack in more constrained cases might be limited if one is unable to develop a query to appropriately bypass GNet. A deeper discussion of the scope or limitations of this specific requirement would be useful.
    *   The effectiveness is primarily demonstrated on image-based tasks.  The generalization of these attacks to other types of deep learning models (e.g., NLP models) or other watermarking schemes is not thoroughly explored.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could trigger further research into more resilient box-free watermarking techniques and improved API security measures.
    *   It could inform the design of more secure deployment strategies for deep learning models, particularly those used as cloud services.
    *   It would encourage the adoption of stronger defenses that are less susceptible to reverse engineering attacks.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Score: 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by identifying and demonstrating a practical vulnerability in a relatively recent watermarking approach for DNNs. The novelty lies in the specific combination of query-based reverse engineering and the exploitation of inherent properties of the watermarking method. The work is well-executed, technically sound, and provides compelling experimental evidence. While the proposed countermeasures could be discussed in more detail, the overall impact on the field of model security and intellectual property protection is significant. The caveats and assumptions about the ability to bypass GNet limit its scope somewhat, as do the current focus on image-based tasks. However, the quality of the work and clarity of the contribution are high.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### NWaaS: Nonintrusive Watermarking as a Service for X-to-Image DNN
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18036v1)
- **Authors**: Haonan An, Guang Hua, Yu Guo, Hangcheng Cao, Susanto Rahardja, Yuguang Fang
- **Abstract**: The intellectual property of deep neural network (DNN) models can be protected with DNN watermarking, which embeds copyright watermarks into model parameters (white-box), model behavior (black-box), or model outputs (box-free), and the watermarks can be subsequently extracted to verify model ownership or detect model theft. Despite recent advances, these existing methods are inherently intrusive, as they either modify the model parameters or alter the structure. This natural intrusiveness raises concerns about watermarking-induced shifts in model behavior and the additional cost of fine-tuning, further exacerbated by the rapidly growing model size. As a result, model owners are often reluctant to adopt DNN watermarking in practice, which limits the development of practical Watermarking as a Service (WaaS) systems. To address this issue, we introduce Nonintrusive Watermarking as a Service (NWaaS), a novel trustless paradigm designed for X-to-Image models, in which we hypothesize that with the model untouched, an owner-defined watermark can still be extracted from model outputs. Building on this concept, we propose ShadowMark, a concrete implementation of NWaaS which addresses critical deployment challenges by establishing a robust and nonintrusive side channel in the protected model's black-box API, leveraging a key encoder and a watermark decoder. It is significantly distinctive from existing solutions by attaining the so-called absolute fidelity and being applicable to different DNN architectures, while being also robust against existing attacks, eliminating the fidelity-robustness trade-off. Extensive experiments on image-to-image, noise-to-image, noise-and-text-to-image, and text-to-image models, demonstrate the efficacy and practicality of ShadowMark for real-world deployment of nonintrusive DNN watermarking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "NWaaS: Nonintrusive Watermarking as a Service for X-to-Image DNN".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new paradigm for DNN watermarking called Nonintrusive Watermarking as a Service (NWaaS).  It addresses the limitations of existing watermarking methods, which are inherently intrusive by modifying model parameters or architecture, leading to fidelity loss, unpredictable behavior changes, and trust issues. NWaaS aims to embed watermarks without modifying the original model, thereby achieving perfect fidelity and operating in a trustless environment.  The paper proposes a concrete implementation of NWaaS called ShadowMark, a key-driven framework for X-to-Image models.  ShadowMark leverages a key encoder and watermark decoder to establish a side channel within the model's API, allowing extraction of owner-defined watermarks from outputs without model modification. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness and practicality of ShadowMark through extensive experiments on various X-to-Image models and analyze its robustness and security.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the paradigm shift from intrusive to nonintrusive DNN watermarking, particularly in the context of a Watermarking as a Service. The idea of extracting watermarks without modifying the model itself is significant. The concept of using key-driven input to side channel output watermarking also represents an approach not commonly seen in the related work, although the underlying principle of using model outputs related to specific inputs to reveal the watermark is aligned with black-box watermarking.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a critical problem preventing the widespread adoption of DNN watermarking: the risks and costs associated with modifying large, complex models. If NWaaS can truly provide effective watermarking without altering the model, it removes a major barrier to deployment. The "trustless" aspect is also significant, as it addresses concerns about relying on third-party watermarking providers.  The implications for intellectual property protection and the enablement of practical WaaS systems are substantial.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of existing watermarking techniques and the challenges hindering WaaS adoption.
    *   **Conceptual Framework:** The NWaaS paradigm is well-defined, and the design requirements for a trustless and nonintrusive system are clearly outlined.
    *   **Technical Approach:** ShadowMark is a reasonably sound and well-described instantiation of the NWaaS paradigm. The approach is creative and the design decisions are properly motivated.
    *   **Experimental Evaluation:** The paper includes a diverse set of experiments on various X-to-Image models, demonstrating the applicability and effectiveness of ShadowMark. The analysis of robustness against model stealing and security against key ambiguity is valuable.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Generality:** The method is demonstrated on X-to-Image models. It is not clear how it can be transferred to non-image outputs or other DNN architecture types.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** While the "trustless" aspect is emphasized, the requirement of needing to define the trigger key and training the encoder/decoder needs the model owner to keep its secrets securely. Furthermore, the performance of training the side channel model *D* is also critical.
    *   **Detailed Threat Model:** The attacker model could be extended to consider more sophisticated attacks that try to reverse-engineer D or analyze the relationship between key and output.
    *   **Scalability and Efficiency:** The paper provides limited discussion on the scalability and computational costs associated with training and deploying the ShadowMark system, especially for very large models.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by promoting a new direction for DNN watermarking, one that prioritizes non-intrusiveness and trust. If the method proves to be robust and scalable, it could pave the way for wider adoption of DNN watermarking in practical applications. The core idea could inspire new research into non-intrusive techniques and side-channel based model protection.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant and well-motivated contribution to the field of DNN watermarking. It tackles a critical practical problem with a novel and reasonably well-executed approach.  However, the generality of the approach and aspects of robustness could be improved in order to justify an exceptional mark.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### GrAInS: Gradient-based Attribution for Inference-Time Steering of LLMs and VLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18043v1)
- **Authors**: Duy Nguyen, Archiki Prasad, Elias Stengel-Eskin, Mohit Bansal
- **Abstract**: Inference-time steering methods offer a lightweight alternative to fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) by modifying internal activations at test time without updating model weights. However, most existing approaches rely on fixed, global intervention vectors, overlook the causal influence of individual input tokens, and fail to leverage informative gradients from the model's logits, particularly in multimodal settings where visual and textual inputs contribute unevenly. To address these limitations, we introduce GrAInS, an inference-time steering approach that operates across both language-only and vision-language models and tasks. GrAInS uses contrastive, gradient-based attribution via Integrated Gradients to identify the top-k most influential tokens, both positively and negatively attributed based on their contribution to preferred versus dispreferred outputs. These tokens are then used to construct directional steering vectors that capture semantic shifts from undesirable to desirable behavior. During inference, GrAInS adjusts hidden activations at transformer layers guided by token-level attribution signals, and normalizes activations to preserve representational scale. This enables fine-grained, interpretable, and modular control over model behavior, without retraining or auxiliary supervision. Empirically, GrAInS consistently outperforms both fine-tuning and existing steering baselines: it achieves a 13.22% accuracy gain on TruthfulQA using Llama-3.1-8B, reduces hallucination rates on MMHal-Bench from 0.624 to 0.514 with LLaVA-1.6-7B, and improves alignment win rates on SPA-VL by 8.11%, all while preserving the model's fluency and general capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GRAINS: Gradient-based Attribution for Inference-Time Steering of LLMs and VLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GRAINS, a novel inference-time steering approach for Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs). GRAINS addresses limitations in existing steering methods by leveraging contrastive, gradient-based attribution (specifically, Integrated Gradients) to identify the most influential input tokens (both positive and negative contributors to the output). It constructs directional steering vectors based on these tokens, adjusting hidden activations at transformer layers during inference. GRAINS normalizes activations to preserve representational scale and claims to offer fine-grained, interpretable, and modular control over model behavior without retraining or auxiliary supervision. The authors demonstrate empirically that GRAINS outperforms fine-tuning and existing steering baselines across several tasks involving truthfulness, hallucination reduction, and alignment, while preserving model fluency and general capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the intelligent combination of existing techniques (inference-time steering, Integrated Gradients, PCA) and their application in a more token-sensitive and directional manner compared to previous work. While each individual component is not new, the synthesis is unique and addresses a clear gap in the field: the lack of fine-grained control in steering LLMs and VLMs based on individual token importance. The contrastive approach (steering away from negative influences and toward positive ones) and the preservation of representational scale are also noteworthy. However, the incremental nature of improvement suggests that the novelty, though present, is not revolutionary.

* **Significance:** The paper has good significance.  Inference-time steering is a highly relevant area, offering a computationally efficient alternative to fine-tuning. GRAINS addresses important failure modes of LLMs and VLMs, such as hallucinations and safety alignment, which are significant concerns for real-world deployment. The empirical results, showing consistent improvements over strong baselines (including fine-tuning), support the method's effectiveness. The interpretability aspect (identifying key tokens influencing the model's behavior) is also valuable for understanding and debugging these models. Finally, the approach's applicability across both LLMs and VLMs increases its potential impact.  However, the dependence on Integrated Gradients introduces a computational overhead compared to simpler steering methods. The practical benefits might be limited in extremely resource-constrained scenarios. Furthermore, the gains, while consistent, are sometimes modest, suggesting that there's still room for improvement.  The reliance on a steering dataset may also limit the generalizability to scenarios without suitable positive/negative examples.

* **Strengths:**
    * Strong empirical evaluation across diverse tasks and models (both LLMs and VLMs).
    * Clear problem definition and motivation.
    * Interpretable steering mechanism.
    * Demonstrates preservation of general capabilities.
    * Publicly available code.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The improvements, while consistent, are not always dramatically larger than baselines.
    * Dependence on Integrated Gradients introduces a computational cost.
    * Relies on contrastive examples which is not always readily available.
    * PCA can obscure token level differences in impact

* **Potential Influence:**  GRAINS has the potential to influence research in inference-time steering and model alignment. It could inspire future work on more sophisticated attribution-guided steering mechanisms or methods to reduce the computational cost of using gradient-based attribution. The interpretable nature of the method could also contribute to a better understanding of how LLMs and VLMs make decisions. It provides a strong baseline for subsequent work in fine-grained, attribution-based control of these models.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** GRAINS presents a novel and effective approach to inference-time steering, addressing limitations of prior work with a clever combination of existing techniques. The empirical results are compelling, demonstrating consistent improvements across various tasks and models. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, and the computational cost (due to Integrated Gradients) and dependence on positive/negative examples represent practical limitations. These aspects prevent a higher score. Overall, it's a solid contribution that advances the state-of-the-art in inference-time model control and has good potential to influence future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Synthetic Data Generation for Phrase Break Prediction with Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18044v1)
- **Authors**: Hoyeon Lee, Sejung Son, Ye-Eun Kang, Jong-Hwan Kim
- **Abstract**: Current approaches to phrase break prediction address crucial prosodic aspects of text-to-speech systems but heavily rely on vast human annotations from audio or text, incurring significant manual effort and cost. Inherent variability in the speech domain, driven by phonetic factors, further complicates acquiring consistent, high-quality data. Recently, large language models (LLMs) have shown success in addressing data challenges in NLP by generating tailored synthetic data while reducing manual annotation needs. Motivated by this, we explore leveraging LLM to generate synthetic phrase break annotations, addressing the challenges of both manual annotation and speech-related tasks by comparing with traditional annotations and assessing effectiveness across multiple languages. Our findings suggest that LLM-based synthetic data generation effectively mitigates data challenges in phrase break prediction and highlights the potential of LLMs as a viable solution for the speech domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate synthetic data for phrase break prediction in text-to-speech (TTS) systems. The authors argue that traditional methods of annotating phrase breaks (audio-oriented and text-oriented) are costly and time-consuming, especially when applied to multiple languages. They propose a system that leverages LLMs to automatically generate these annotations, demonstrating that the synthetic data is comparable in quality to human-generated data. The study compares LLM-generated annotations to human annotations in English, French, and Spanish and shows that models trained on synthetic data can achieve performance comparable to or exceeding that of models trained on human-annotated data. The paper also investigates the use of cross-lingual transfer to reduce annotation costs in resource-scarce languages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to generate synthetic data for *phrase break prediction* is somewhat novel, though LLMs have been used for synthetic data generation in other NLP tasks. The application to the specific problem of phrase break annotation is where the novelty lies. The study explores different few-shot learning settings and cross-lingual transfer techniques, which add a layer of methodological contribution.

**Significance:** The potential impact of this work is significant. If LLMs can effectively generate high-quality phrase break annotations, it could drastically reduce the cost and effort associated with building TTS systems, particularly for low-resource languages. It could also lead to more consistent and standardized annotation practices, overcoming some of the inconsistencies inherent in human annotation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies a real-world problem in TTS and proposes a plausible solution.
*   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments are thorough and well-designed, including comparisons with different types of human annotations and evaluations across multiple languages.
*   **Clear results:** The results are presented clearly and demonstrate the potential of LLMs for synthetic data generation in this context.
*   **Cross-lingual exploration:** The study investigates cross-lingual transfer, which is important for addressing data scarcity in many languages.
*   **Addresses a speech-specific challenge:** Previous LLM applications for synthetic data have focused primarily on text-based tasks, so this paper expands the application of LLMs to the speech domain.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Task Complexity:** Phrase Break Prediction is a classification problem. The novelty of LLM-based data generation is in the context of the specific *task* but not at a *theoretical* level. The novelty of using LLMs for data generation is already established in literature.

*   **Dependence on a powerful LLM:** The approach relies heavily on the capabilities of a powerful LLM (GPT-4o mini). It is unclear how well this approach would generalize to less capable or smaller LLMs. The prompt engineering is also likely to be sensitive, so the exact implementation matters.

*   **Metrics used:** Human score could be subjective; also there may not have been a correlation between the F1 score (objective measure) and human preference (subjective measure).

*   **Limited evaluation dataset size:** They used only 1000 utterances each for the target languages to evaluate LLM annotation quality. Considering real world applications, this number may be too small to estimate performance accurately.

*   **The paper lacks information about the training datasets for English, French, and Spanish. How similar or different are the datasets?**: Without this information, readers have little information about the amount of variation.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable application of LLMs and demonstrates promising results, it's important to temper enthusiasm. The core idea is an adaptation of existing LLM techniques to a specific domain. The experiments are solid, but the dependence on a powerful LLM and potentially brittle prompt engineering limit the immediate impact and generalizability of the work. Therefore, I'm assigning a score of **7**. The work is a worthwhile contribution, but the significance of its generalizability, dependence on specific LLMs, and other metric-based considerations justify this rating.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### RECALLED: An Unbounded Resource Consumption Attack on Large Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18053v1)
- **Authors**: Haoran Gao, Yuanhe Zhang, Zhenhong Zhou, Lei Jiang, Fanyu Meng, Yujia Xiao, Kun Wang, Yang Liu, Junlan Feng
- **Abstract**: Resource Consumption Attacks (RCAs) have emerged as a significant threat to the deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs). With the integration of vision modalities, additional attack vectors exacerbate the risk of RCAs in large vision-language models (LVLMs). However, existing red-teaming studies have largely overlooked visual inputs as a potential attack surface, resulting in insufficient mitigation strategies against RCAs in LVLMs. To address this gap, we propose RECALLED (\textbf{RE}source \textbf{C}onsumption \textbf{A}ttack on \textbf{L}arge Vision-\textbf{L}anguag\textbf{E} Mo\textbf{D}els), the first approach for exploiting visual modalities to trigger unbounded RCAs red-teaming. First, we present \textit{Vision Guided Optimization}, a fine-grained pixel-level optimization, to obtain \textit{Output Recall} adversarial perturbations, which can induce repeating output. Then, we inject the perturbations into visual inputs, triggering unbounded generations to achieve the goal of RCAs. Additionally, we introduce \textit{Multi-Objective Parallel Losses} to generate universal attack templates and resolve optimization conflicts when intending to implement parallel attacks. Empirical results demonstrate that RECALLED increases service response latency by over 26 $\uparrow$, resulting in an additional 20\% increase in GPU utilization and memory consumption. Our study exposes security vulnerabilities in LVLMs and establishes a red-teaming framework that can facilitate future defense development against RCAs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "RECALLED: An Unbounded Resource Consumption Attack on Large Vision-Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RECALLED, a novel red-teaming approach to expose resource consumption vulnerabilities in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). RECALLED leverages visual input to trigger unbounded output generation, leading to increased inference latency, GPU utilization, and memory consumption. The approach includes Vision Guided Optimization to craft adversarial visual perturbations targeting output recall and a Multi-Objective Parallel Loss function to improve the universality and efficiency of the attacks. The effectiveness of RECALLED is demonstrated through experiments on state-of-the-art LVLMs, showing significant increases in service latency and resource usage. The paper also explores potential defense mechanisms and analyzes the attack's impact on model stability and covertness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the exploitation of visual inputs as an attack surface for resource consumption in LVLMs. Existing work primarily focuses on text-based attacks, making RECALLED a significant step in identifying and addressing vulnerabilities arising from multimodal input processing. The introduction of Vision Guided Optimization for crafting adversarial visual perturbations tailored to trigger unbounded output repetition is also a novel contribution. The Multi-Objective Parallel Loss function contributes to the practical application of the attack by facilitating universal template generation.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from highlighting a critical security gap in LVLMs. Resource consumption attacks pose a serious threat to the deployment and reliability of these models, and RECALLED demonstrates the potential for visual inputs to exacerbate these vulnerabilities. By providing a red-teaming framework for identifying and analyzing RCAs in LVLMs, the paper contributes to the development of more robust and secure multimodal models. The defense strategies proposed, while not fully optimized, provide a starting point for future research in mitigating these attacks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of resource consumption attacks in LVLMs and motivates the need for addressing vulnerabilities related to visual inputs.
    *   **Well-Designed Methodology:** The RECALLED approach is well-designed and incorporates novel techniques for generating adversarial visual perturbations and improving attack universality.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough experimental evaluation of RECALLED's effectiveness on multiple state-of-the-art LVLMs, demonstrating its ability to trigger unbounded output generation and increase resource consumption.
    *   **Analysis of Underlying Mechanisms:** The paper provides valuable insights into the underlying mechanisms that contribute to the vulnerability of LVLMs to resource consumption attacks, particularly regarding the model's tendency to enter self-reinforcing generation loops.
    *   **Defense Strategy Exploration:** While preliminary, the exploration of defense strategies offers valuable directions for future research in mitigating RCAs in LVLMs.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Defense Evaluation:** The evaluation of the proposed defense strategies is limited, and the trade-offs between security and usability are not fully explored.
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** The experimental results may be influenced by the specific datasets used for evaluation. Generalizing the findings to other datasets and real-world scenarios requires further investigation.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides empirical evidence for the effectiveness of RECALLED, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of the underlying mechanisms and attack surface would strengthen the contribution.
    *   **Ethical Concerns:** The paper should acknowledge the potential for misuse of the presented attack techniques and emphasize the importance of responsible disclosure and ethical considerations. A better explanation of safe deployment guidelines could also improve the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in several areas, including:

    *   Security and robustness of LVLMs.
    *   Red-teaming and adversarial testing of multimodal models.
    *   Development of defense mechanisms against resource consumption attacks.
    *   Understanding the interplay between visual and textual modalities in LVLM vulnerabilities.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel approach to a relevant problem, there's room for improvement regarding theoretical analysis, limitations, the deployment of defense strategies, and ethical considerations. RECALLED is a significant and impactful research, thus, a score of 7/10 is warranted.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Privacy-Preserving Synthetic Review Generation with Diverse Writing Styles Using LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18055v1)
- **Authors**: Tevin Atwal, Chan Nam Tieu, Yefeng Yuan, Zhan Shi, Yuhong Liu, Liang Cheng
- **Abstract**: The increasing use of synthetic data generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) presents both opportunities and challenges in data-driven applications. While synthetic data provides a cost-effective, scalable alternative to real-world data to facilitate model training, its diversity and privacy risks remain underexplored. Focusing on text-based synthetic data, we propose a comprehensive set of metrics to quantitatively assess the diversity (i.e., linguistic expression, sentiment, and user perspective), and privacy (i.e., re-identification risk and stylistic outliers) of synthetic datasets generated by several state-of-the-art LLMs. Experiment results reveal significant limitations in LLMs' capabilities in generating diverse and privacy-preserving synthetic data. Guided by the evaluation results, a prompt-based approach is proposed to enhance the diversity of synthetic reviews while preserving reviewer privacy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the challenge of generating diverse and privacy-preserving synthetic product review data using Large Language Models (LLMs). It proposes a comprehensive evaluation framework comprised of metrics to assess both diversity (lexical, semantic, and sentiment) and privacy risks (identifiable content and stylistic uniqueness). The framework is applied to real-world Amazon reviews and synthetic datasets generated by state-of-the-art LLMs (GPT-4o and Claude 3.7 Sonnet).  The results reveal limitations in LLMs' capabilities regarding both diversity and privacy. To mitigate these limitations, the authors introduce an iterative prompt-based approach that dynamically adjusts the generation instructions based on evaluation feedback, aiming to enhance diversity while preserving reviewer privacy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Comprehensive Evaluation Framework:** The paper's main strength lies in its creation of a detailed and multifaceted evaluation framework.  Quantifying both diversity *and* privacy across various dimensions (lexical richness, semantic diversity, sentiment alignment, identifiable content, stylistic outliers) is a significant contribution. The framework fills a gap in the literature, providing researchers with a toolkit to critically assess synthetic data generated by LLMs.
    * **Practical Problem Addressed:** The problem of generating useful, diverse, and private synthetic data is critical for responsible AI development. This work tackles a real-world issue with a clearly defined application area (product reviews).
    * **Iterative Prompting Approach:** The dynamic prompt optimization is a valuable contribution. Adapting the prompt based on metric failures is a smart strategy for guiding LLMs towards desired outputs without manual intervention. This moves beyond simple prompting techniques and demonstrates a more sophisticated approach to synthetic data generation.
    * **Empirical Validation:** Applying the framework to both real-world and synthetically generated data is commendable. The comparison of different LLMs highlights their strengths and weaknesses, providing valuable insights for practitioners.  The use of Amazon reviews offers a benchmark against which to assess the synthetic data.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Metric Choices:** While comprehensive, the metric choices aren't radically new.  Many are established NLP measures (N-grams, Sentiment Analysis). The novelty is more in the combination and application to this specific synthetic data generation problem. The reliance on average MST edge length is questionable; it could be influenced by factors other than just semantic diversity.
    * **Prompt Engineering Specifics:** The paper lacks detailed specifics on *how* the prompts were designed and refined.  The adaptive approach is promising, but the specifics of the initial prompt pool and the rules for updating them are somewhat vague. The design of prompt updates to target failed evaluations is also unclear. Without these details, the reproducibility of the approach is limited.
    * **Limited Generalizability:** The focus is very specific to *product reviews*. It's not clear how well the proposed metrics and prompting strategies would generalize to other text domains or data types.
    * **Privacy Risk Discussion:** The analysis of privacy risks, while present, isn't deeply explored. The stylistic outlier detection is a good start, but further investigation into re-identification attacks and the effectiveness of the proposed approach in truly preventing memorization by downstream models would strengthen the paper.
    * **Lack of comparative approach:** How does this framework compare to other frameworks that generate synthetic data in regards to diversity and privacy?

* **Novelty:**

The combination of the diversity of metrics and privacy risk identification metrics together with an iterative prompt-based enhancement approach is the most novel component. While individual components exist, the integration and application to the LLM synthetic data context are novel.

* **Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in providing a practical framework for evaluating and improving synthetic data generation with LLMs. The combination of diversity and privacy metrics allows researchers to critically evaluate synthetic data and can encourage the development of more responsible data generation practices.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign the paper a score of **7**.

* **Rationale:** The paper makes a solid contribution by providing a comprehensive evaluation framework and a dynamic prompting approach. The weaknesses lie in the limited depth of analysis of some of the metric selections, missing implementation details, and the narrow focus. However, the paper addresses an important problem with a practical solution that is well-validated empirically.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### BokehDiff: Neural Lens Blur with One-Step Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18060v1)
- **Authors**: Chengxuan Zhu, Qingnan Fan, Qi Zhang, Jinwei Chen, Huaqi Zhang, Chao Xu, Boxin Shi
- **Abstract**: We introduce BokehDiff, a novel lens blur rendering method that achieves physically accurate and visually appealing outcomes, with the help of generative diffusion prior. Previous methods are bounded by the accuracy of depth estimation, generating artifacts in depth discontinuities. Our method employs a physics-inspired self-attention module that aligns with the image formation process, incorporating depth-dependent circle of confusion constraint and self-occlusion effects. We adapt the diffusion model to the one-step inference scheme without introducing additional noise, and achieve results of high quality and fidelity. To address the lack of scalable paired data, we propose to synthesize photorealistic foregrounds with transparency with diffusion models, balancing authenticity and scene diversity.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents BokehDiff, a new method for rendering lens blur that utilizes a generative diffusion prior. It addresses the limitations of existing techniques, particularly those related to inaccuracies in depth estimation, which lead to artifacts near depth discontinuities. BokehDiff incorporates a physics-inspired self-attention module that is closely aligned with the image formation process, allowing for the integration of depth-dependent effects such as circles of confusion and self-occlusion. By modifying the standard diffusion model into a one-step inference approach, the authors manage to eliminate additional noise while achieving high-quality and high-fidelity outputs. To tackle the challenge of limited scalable paired data, the authors also propose a method for synthesizing photorealistic transparent foregrounds using diffusion models, balancing authenticity with scene diversity. **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant advancement in the area of image rendering, particularly in achieving high-fidelity lens blur effects without the common artifacts associated with depth estimation errors. By leveraging a physics-inspired approach, the self-attention module represented in BokehDiff is notable for its alignment with real-world imaging physics. The adaptation of diffusion models for one-step inference is innovative and adds to the existing knowledge base in generative models, offering a streamlined alternative to traditional multi-step processes. **Significance:** BokehDiff effectively addresses practical issues faced in rendering applications, which is paramount for fields such as computer graphics, virtual reality, and augmented reality. The introduction of scalable synthetic data generation through diffusion methods could also enhance diversity and authenticity in training datasets, which is often a bottleneck in machine learning applications. This potential impact on data synthesis could lead to broader implications in various domains, from visual effects in film production to digital art creation. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovation in Methodology:** Employs a novel self-attention mechanism during the rendering process. 2. **High-Quality Results:** Produces results characterized by physical realism and visual appeal. 3. **Addressing Data Limitations:** Contributes to solving the problem of insufficient training data through synthetic generation. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Complexity of Implementation:** While inventive, the enhanced self-attention strategy may increase the complexity of implementation in practical applications. 2. **Evaluation Metrics:** The paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of evaluation metrics and how they compare with existing benchmarks in the field. 3. **Generalizability:** It remains to be seen how well this method generalizes across various types of scenes and photographic conditions. **Potential Influence:** BokehDiff's contributions hold promise for redefining how lens blur is rendered in computational imaging, pushing the boundaries of realism in generated images. Its impacts could resonate across interactive media, enhancing user experience immensely while opening avenues for future research. Given these considerations, I assign the paper a **score of 8**. The work presents notable advancements and significant applications, but imposes complexity that might hinder widespread adoption and offers a somewhat limited discussion on broader implications.  **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### TELEVAL: A Dynamic Benchmark Designed for Spoken Language Models in Chinese Interactive Scenarios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18061v1)
- **Authors**: Zehan Li, Hongjie Chen, Yuxin Zhang, Jing Zhou, Xuening Wang, Hang Lv, Mengjie Du, Yaodong Song, Jie Lian, Jian Kang, Jie Li, Yongxiang Li, Zhongjiang He, Xuelong Li
- **Abstract**: Spoken language models (SLMs) have seen rapid progress in recent years, along with the development of numerous benchmarks for evaluating their performance. However, most existing benchmarks primarily focus on evaluating whether SLMs can perform complex tasks comparable to those tackled by large language models (LLMs), often failing to align with how users naturally interact in real-world conversational scenarios. In this paper, we propose TELEVAL, a dynamic benchmark specifically designed to evaluate SLMs' effectiveness as conversational agents in realistic Chinese interactive settings. TELEVAL defines three evaluation dimensions: Explicit Semantics, Paralinguistic and Implicit Semantics, and System Abilities. It adopts a dialogue format consistent with real-world usage and evaluates text and audio outputs separately. TELEVAL particularly focuses on the model's ability to extract implicit cues from user speech and respond appropriately without additional instructions. Our experiments demonstrate that despite recent progress, existing SLMs still have considerable room for improvement in natural conversational tasks. We hope that TELEVAL can serve as a user-centered evaluation framework that directly reflects the user experience and contributes to the development of more capable dialogue-oriented SLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TELEVAL: A Dynamic Benchmark Designed for Spoken Language Models in Chinese Interactive Scenarios":

**Summary**

The paper introduces TELEVAL, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the performance of spoken language models (SLMs) in realistic Chinese interactive scenarios.  The benchmark addresses a perceived gap in existing benchmarks, which often focus on general language understanding or complex tasks without fully capturing the nuances of natural human-computer interactions. TELEVAL emphasizes a user-centered approach, focusing on the model's ability to extract implicit cues from user speech (like emotion, tone, dialect) and respond appropriately, even without explicit instructions. The benchmark is organized around three evaluation dimensions: Explicit Semantics, Paralinguistic and Implicit Semantics, and System Capabilities. It features a diverse set of tasks and datasets and evaluates both text and audio outputs separately.  Experimental results show that existing SLMs, even advanced ones, still have room for improvement in achieving truly natural and nuanced conversational interactions.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents a valuable and timely contribution to the field. While existing SLM benchmarks are plentiful, their applicability to realistic, interactive scenarios is often limited. TELEVAL distinguishes itself by specifically targeting these nuances, focusing on implicit cues and appropriate responses. The inclusion of dialect-specific tasks and the separate evaluation of audio outputs are also novel aspects.  The explicit attempt to move beyond just correctness to naturalness and human-likeness is commendable.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in TELEVAL's potential to drive the development of more user-friendly and intuitive spoken dialogue systems. By focusing on naturalness and the ability to interpret implicit cues, the benchmark can push SLMs beyond simply understanding commands to engaging in truly conversational exchanges. The detailed evaluation dimensions and tasks provide a roadmap for researchers to improve specific aspects of their models' conversational abilities.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **User-centric design:** The emphasis on realistic interaction scenarios and user experience is a significant strength.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation dimensions:** The three evaluation dimensions provide a structured and thorough assessment of SLM capabilities.
    *   **Focus on implicit cues:** The emphasis on extracting and responding to implicit cues is crucial for natural conversation.
    *   **Separation of text and audio evaluation:** This approach reduces errors from ASR systems and allows for a more accurate assessment of the SLM.
    *   **Detailed methodology:** The data construction, evaluation metrics, and LLM-as-judge procedures are well-defined.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM-as-judge Reliance:** While the paper mentions mitigating biases in using LLMs for evaluation, this method inherently introduces a degree of subjectivity and potential biases. This remains a common limitation in many NLP benchmarks.
    *   **Data Synthesis vs. Real Data:** The reliance on synthetic speech data for many tasks, while practical, might not fully capture the complexities of real human speech and conversational patterns. However, the use of real human recordings for emotional expression and NSV tasks somewhat mitigates this.
    *   **Limited Model Coverage:**  The experiments only cover a specific set of models. The field is rapidly evolving, and including more recent, state-of-the-art models would further strengthen the benchmark.
    *   **Generalizability beyond Chinese Scenarios:** Although the focus on Chinese interactive scenarios is a strength, it also somewhat limits the broader applicability of the benchmark. Some tasks related to paralinguistics might be applicable to other languages with necessary adjustments.
    *   **Acoustic Robustness:** The methods for generating the test data are borrowed from other papers, and the details given in Appendix C do not clearly state how the distortions are generated, so there is some uncertainty surrounding how relevant they are to this area.

*   **Potential Influence:** TELEVAL has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating SLMs in Chinese interactive scenarios. The results and insights generated using TELEVAL can guide future research and development in this area.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**  TELEVAL represents a significant and novel contribution to the field of SLM evaluation. It fills a crucial gap by focusing on the nuances of realistic interactive scenarios and emphasizing the importance of responding to implicit cues. The benchmark is well-designed, comprehensive, and has the potential to drive progress in developing more natural and user-friendly spoken dialogue systems. The score reflects the paper's novelty, significance, and strengths, tempered by the inherent limitations of relying on LLMs for evaluation, the use of synthetic speech data, and the limited model coverage.  A higher score would be warranted with the inclusion of more state-of-the-art models and more stringent mitigation of potential LLM-induced biases. Also, the relevance of the Acoustic Robustness is uncertain without a clearer statement of methodology.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Group Sequence Policy Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18071v1)
- **Authors**: Chujie Zheng, Shixuan Liu, Mingze Li, Xiong-Hui Chen, Bowen Yu, Chang Gao, Kai Dang, Yuqiong Liu, Rui Men, An Yang, Jingren Zhou, Junyang Lin
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), our stable, efficient, and performant reinforcement learning algorithm for training large language models. Unlike previous algorithms that adopt token-level importance ratios, GSPO defines the importance ratio based on sequence likelihood and performs sequence-level clipping, rewarding, and optimization. We demonstrate that GSPO achieves superior training efficiency and performance compared to the GRPO algorithm, notably stabilizes Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) RL training, and has the potential for simplifying the design of RL infrastructure. These merits of GSPO have contributed to the remarkable improvements in the latest Qwen3 models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), a reinforcement learning (RL) algorithm designed for training large language models (LLMs). GSPO distinguishes itself from prior approaches like GRPO by defining the importance ratio at the sequence level (rather than token level) based on sequence likelihood, performing sequence-level clipping, and employing sequence-level reward and optimization.  The authors argue that this sequence-level approach addresses instability issues inherent in token-level methods when training very large models, particularly Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models. They demonstrate that GSPO stabilizes MoE training, improves training efficiency and performance compared to GRPO, and has contributed to improvements in the Qwen3 model family. They also claim potential simplifications of RL infrastructure using GSPO.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core idea of moving from token-level to sequence-level importance weighting in RL for LLMs constitutes a significant step forward. The authors clearly identify the instability issues of existing methods such as GRPO and provide a compelling argument for why token-level weighting is problematic in the context of long sequences and large models. While importance sampling is a well-established technique, its adaptation to sequence-level for the RL fine-tuning of LLMs presents a novel approach. The token level variant is interesting, but more of a tweak than a new contribution.

* **Significance:** Addressing the instability issues in RL fine-tuning of LLMs is crucial for scaling these models to even greater capabilities.  The ability to train MoE models without complex stabilization strategies (like Routing Replay) is also highly significant, as MoE models are critical for scaling LLMs. The potential for simplifying RL infrastructure by reducing sensitivity to precision discrepancies offers practical advantages. If GSPO consistently delivers on its claims, it could become a standard RL algorithm for LLMs. The claim that GSPO contributed to the Qwen3 updates is a substantial endorsement.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches, particularly GRPO, and provides a strong justification for the need for a new algorithm.
    * **Sound Theoretical Basis:** The sequence-level importance weighting is grounded in the principles of importance sampling, lending credibility to the approach.
    * **Empirical Validation:**  The experimental results demonstrate the superiority of GSPO over GRPO in terms of stability, efficiency, and performance. The application to the latest Qwen3 models provides real-world relevance.
    * **Practical Benefits:** The paper highlights the potential for simplifying MoE training and RL infrastructure, which are valuable benefits for practitioners.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:** While comparing GSPO to GRPO is valuable, more ablation studies could strengthen the analysis. For example, it would be good to see the effects of clipping range adjustments on GSPO itself or removing the length normalization in the sequence likelihood calculation.
    * **Reproducibility:**  As with much LLM research, reproducing the results requires substantial computational resources and access to model architectures. Details of the experimental setup, such as the exact query sets used,  are important for others to build upon the research but may be hard to present.
    * **Generalizability:** While impressive results are shown on Qwen3-30B and the indicated benchmarks, it is important to see GSPO applied and benchmarked on different foundational LLM architectures and use cases.

* **Impact:**  The potential impact is high. If GSPO proves to be a robust and scalable RL algorithm for LLMs, it could significantly accelerate the development of more powerful and capable models. The contributions toward simplification are also important.
*The addition of a token variant while mathematically equivalent, is not a particularly impactful or novel addition.

**Justification for Score:**

GSPO addresses a crucial problem in the scaling of LLMs with RL and presents a sound, well-justified approach. The empirical results are convincing and the potential practical benefits are significant. The paper also suffers from a few areas, where greater effort to study the parameter landscape would have boosted the impact.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Squeeze10-LLM: Squeezing LLMs' Weights by 10 Times via a Staged Mixed-Precision Quantization Method
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18073v1)
- **Authors**: Qingcheng Zhu, Yangyang Ren, Linlin Yang, Mingbao Lin, Yanjing Li, Sheng Xu, Zichao Feng, Haodong Zhu, Yuguang Yang, Juan Zhang, Runqi Wang, Baochang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Deploying large language models (LLMs) is challenging due to their massive parameters and high computational costs. Ultra low-bit quantization can significantly reduce storage and accelerate inference, but extreme compression (i.e., mean bit-width <= 2) often leads to severe performance degradation. To address this, we propose Squeeze10-LLM, effectively "squeezing" 16-bit LLMs' weights by 10 times. Specifically, Squeeze10-LLM is a staged mixed-precision post-training quantization (PTQ) framework and achieves an average of 1.6 bits per weight by quantizing 80% of the weights to 1 bit and 20% to 4 bits. We introduce Squeeze10LLM with two key innovations: Post-Binarization Activation Robustness (PBAR) and Full Information Activation Supervision (FIAS). PBAR is a refined weight significance metric that accounts for the impact of quantization on activations, improving accuracy in low-bit settings. FIAS is a strategy that preserves full activation information during quantization to mitigate cumulative error propagation across layers.
