Existing studies primarily highlight that LLMs are receptive to external information that contradict their parametric knowledge, but little research has been conducted on the direct impact of instruction-tuning on this phenomenon. In our study, we investigate the impact of instruction-tuning on LLM's susceptibility to misinformation. Our analysis reveals that instruction-tuned LLMs are significantly more likely to accept misinformation when it is presented by the user. A comparison with base models shows that instruction-tuning increases reliance on user-provided information, shifting susceptibility from the assistant role to the user role. Furthermore, we explore additional factors influencing misinformation susceptibility, such as the role of the user in prompt structure, misinformation length, and the presence of warnings in the system prompt. Our findings underscore the need for systematic approaches to mitigate unintended consequences of instruction-tuning and enhance the reliability of LLMs in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of instruction-tuning on Large Language Models' (LLMs) susceptibility to misinformation. The authors hypothesize that instruction-tuning, while improving LLMs' ability to follow instructions and reduce harmful outputs, might also increase their dependence on user input, making them more vulnerable to accepting and propagating misinformation. Through experiments using the Farm dataset and several open-source and proprietary LLMs, the study finds that instruction-tuned LLMs are significantly more likely to accept misinformation presented in the user-role compared to base models. The research also explores the impact of misinformation length and the presence of warnings on this susceptibility. The results suggest that instruction-tuning shifts LLMs towards being more user-focused, which can increase the risk of hallucination when users provide incorrect information.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  While the general problem of LLM susceptibility to misinformation is known, the paper offers a novel angle by specifically examining the *causal* role of *instruction-tuning* in increasing this susceptibility. It moves beyond simply identifying the problem, aiming to understand its underlying cause related to a specific LLM training technique. The design of experiments to distinguish between user and assistant roles is also a key methodological contribution. The direct comparison between base and instruction-tuned models further strengthens the novelty of the analysis.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant concern in the development and deployment of LLMs. As instruction-tuned models become increasingly prevalent, understanding their vulnerabilities to misinformation is crucial. The findings highlight a trade-off between instruction-following and factual grounding, suggesting that the alignment process needs to be carefully managed to prevent increased susceptibility to user-provided misinformation. It could guide future research towards developing mitigation strategies for this issue.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear research questions:** The paper formulates specific and testable research questions (RQ1, RQ2, RQ3).
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The experimental design is well-structured, comparing instruction-tuned models with their base versions and varying factors like the user-role and misinformation length. The use of a established dataset (FARM) strengthens the methodology.
    *   **Comprehensive analysis:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of the experimental results, discussing the implications for different models and scenarios.
    *   **Multiple Model Comparison:** The use of several LLMs improves the generalizability of the results.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope in Warning Effects:** The analysis of the effect of warnings on misinformation susceptibility, while informative, could benefit from investigating more sophisticated warning strategies.  The current analysis shows it only has a minimal affect across most open-source models, but it may not be an inherent limitation for all models.
    *   **Model Type Limitation:** Only models within a specific parameter count range (7B-8B) were tested (except GPT-4). The conclusion made may not scale to models with vastly larger or smaller size
    *   **Potential dataset biases:** The paper acknowledges some idiosyncrasies in model responses depending on the source dataset of the FARM dataset.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    I assign a score of **7**.

    *   The paper tackles a very important issue for real-world LLM deployments - **propgation of misinformation**.
    *   The research has strong novelty, but limitations on tested models and scope weakens the generalizability

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Prune&Comp: Free Lunch for Layer-Pruned LLMs via Iterative Pruning with Magnitude Compensation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18212v1)
- **Authors**: Xinrui Chen, Hongxing Zhang, Fanyi Zeng, Yongxian Wei, Yizhi Wang, Xitong Ling, Guanghao Li, Chun Yuan
- **Abstract**: Layer pruning has emerged as a promising technique for compressing large language models (LLMs) while achieving acceleration proportional to the pruning ratio. In this work, we identify that removing any layer induces a significant magnitude gap in hidden states, resulting in substantial performance degradation. To address this issue, we propose Prune&Comp, a novel plug-and-play layer pruning scheme that leverages magnitude compensation to mitigate such gaps in a training-free manner. Specifically, we first estimate the magnitude gap caused by layer removal and then eliminate this gap by rescaling the remaining weights offline, with zero runtime overhead incurred. We further demonstrate the advantages of Prune&Comp through an iterative pruning strategy. When integrated with an iterative prune-and-compensate loop, Prune&Comp consistently enhances existing layer pruning metrics. For instance, when 5 layers of LLaMA-3-8B are pruned using the prevalent block influence metric, Prune&Comp nearly halves the perplexity and retains 93.19\% of the original model's question-answering performance, outperforming the baseline by 4.01%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PRUNE&COMP, a novel training-free layer pruning method for large language models (LLMs). The key idea is to address the performance degradation that occurs when layers are removed by compensating for the magnitude gap induced in the hidden states.  PRUNE&COMP estimates this gap and then rescales the remaining weights offline, incurring no runtime overhead. The paper further enhances this approach by integrating it with an iterative pruning strategy.  Experiments demonstrate that PRUNE&COMP consistently improves the performance of existing layer pruning metrics on various LLMs and benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of compensating for the magnitude gap caused by layer removal is relatively novel within the specific context of LLM layer pruning. While magnitude-based methods exist in general pruning literature, this paper's observation that layer removal *inherently* creates a magnitude gap needing correction, and the method of addressing it without retraining, provides a fresh perspective. The iterative application of this compensation, while not revolutionary, adds a practical dimension.

*   **Significance:** Layer pruning is an essential technique for reducing the computational cost and latency of LLMs. PRUNE&COMP offers a simple and effective way to enhance existing layer pruning methods without requiring retraining, making it practically significant. The consistent performance gains reported across different LLMs, benchmarks, and pruning metrics suggest that PRUNE&COMP is a generalizable and valuable contribution to the field.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Simplicity:** The proposed method is easy to understand and implement, which increases its practical appeal.
    *   **Training-free:**  Avoiding retraining is a major advantage, as it reduces the computational burden and time required for model compression.
    *   **Generalizability:** The method is shown to be effective with multiple pruning metrics, architectures (LLaMA-2, LLaMA-3, Qwen), and evaluation benchmarks.
    *   **No Inference Overhead:** The offline rescaling approach ensures that the method doesn't slow down inference.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of PRUNE&COMP, showing significant improvements in perplexity and question-answering performance.
    *   **Addresses an Intrinsic Problem:** The paper identifies and addresses a fundamental issue (magnitude gap) that is inherent to layer pruning, rather than focusing on metric design alone.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the empirical results are compelling, the paper lacks a deeper theoretical explanation for *why* the magnitude compensation is so effective. More analysis of the hidden state distributions and their impact on subsequent layers could strengthen the argument.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The technique enhances *existing* metrics. It doesn't introduce a ground-breaking new metric or approach to layer identification itself. Its strength lies in improving what is already available.
    *   **Dependency on Calibration Set:** The method relies on a calibration dataset to estimate the magnitude gap.  The paper provides limited analysis on the impact of calibration dataset size and composition.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses solely on layer pruning. Its applicability to other pruning paradigms (e.g., width pruning, attention head pruning) remains unexplored.

*   **Potential Influence:** PRUNE&COMP is likely to be adopted by researchers and practitioners working on LLM compression. Its simplicity and effectiveness make it a valuable addition to the existing toolbox of pruning techniques. The concept of magnitude compensation could also inspire further research into other types of post-pruning correction methods.

*   **Overall:**  The paper provides a practical and valuable contribution to LLM compression. While it is not a revolutionary breakthrough, its simplicity, generality, and strong empirical results make it a significant advance in the field.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper is a solid contribution with clear practical value. It addresses a relevant problem in LLM compression (performance degradation due to layer pruning) with a simple, effective, and training-free method. The strong empirical results across multiple models and benchmarks support the paper's claims. However, the lack of deeper theoretical justification and the incremental nature of the improvement prevent a higher score. The score of 7 reflects that the research presents a worthwhile advance, but it isn't transformative enough to reach the highest tiers.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LEAF: Latent Diffusion with Efficient Encoder Distillation for Aligned Features in Medical Image Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18214v1)
- **Authors**: Qilin Huang, Tianyu Lin, Zhiguang Chen, Fudan Zheng
- **Abstract**: Leveraging the powerful capabilities of diffusion models has yielded quite effective results in medical image segmentation tasks. However, existing methods typically transfer the original training process directly without specific adjustments for segmentation tasks. Furthermore, the commonly used pre-trained diffusion models still have deficiencies in feature extraction. Based on these considerations, we propose LEAF, a medical image segmentation model grounded in latent diffusion models. During the fine-tuning process, we replace the original noise prediction pattern with a direct prediction of the segmentation map, thereby reducing the variance of segmentation results. We also employ a feature distillation method to align the hidden states of the convolutional layers with the features from a transformer-based vision encoder. Experimental results demonstrate that our method enhances the performance of the original diffusion model across multiple segmentation datasets for different disease types. Notably, our approach does not alter the model architecture, nor does it increase the number of parameters or computation during the inference phase, making it highly efficient.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LEAF: Latent Diffusion with Efficient Encoder Distillation for Aligned Features in Medical Image Segmentation":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes LEAF, a method to improve medical image segmentation using latent diffusion models (LDMs). It addresses two key limitations of existing LDM-based segmentation methods: the direct transfer of training processes without segmentation-specific adjustments and deficiencies in feature extraction. LEAF introduces two main components: 1) replacing the original noise prediction with direct segmentation map prediction, reducing variance in results, and 2) a feature distillation method to align convolutional layer features with a transformer-based vision encoder's features.  This alignment enhances feature representation without increasing parameters or computational cost during inference. The authors demonstrate improved performance on multiple medical image segmentation datasets for different disease types. The method is designed to be computationally efficient, not altering the model architecture or increasing the number of parameters during inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of replacing noise prediction with direct segmentation map prediction is a relatively simple yet potentially effective adjustment tailored for segmentation tasks.  While LDMs have been applied to segmentation before, this targeted modification is noteworthy. The feature distillation method, while inspired by prior work (REPA), is adapted for medical image segmentation, aligning U-Net features with a pre-trained visual encoder. This specific application in LDMs for medical imaging shows novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its ability to improve the performance of LDMs for medical image segmentation while maintaining efficiency. Medical image segmentation is a crucial task with significant clinical applications. LEAF's efficient feature alignment is particularly valuable as it doesn't add computational overhead during inference.  The results show demonstrable performance gains over existing methods across multiple datasets, suggesting a degree of generalizability.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Targeted Problem Definition:** The paper correctly identifies limitations of directly applying LDMs to segmentation and addresses them with specific modifications.
    *   **Efficient Design:** Maintaining computational efficiency is a key strength, making the method more practical for real-world applications. The fact that inference isn't burdened with additional computation is a major advantage.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experiments clearly demonstrate performance improvements over existing methods across diverse datasets, providing solid evidence for the effectiveness of LEAF.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies isolate the contribution of each component (prediction type and feature alignment), strengthening the claims about their individual and combined impact.
    *   **Stability Analysis**: The stability experiment further demonstrates robustness, alleviating concerns about randomness affecting the reported gains.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the method is effective, the improvements are somewhat incremental. LEAF leverages existing architectures and pre-trained models and combines them with tailored strategies. The reliance on pre-trained models (e.g., DINOv2) could limit its broader applicability if those models have biases or perform poorly on specific medical image domains.
    *   **Limited Architectural Exploration:** The paper primarily focuses on modifying the training process and feature alignment rather than exploring novel LDM architectures tailored for segmentation.

*   **Potential Impact:**
    *   The paper contributes a practical and effective approach to improving LDM-based medical image segmentation. Its efficiency and strong performance could make it attractive for clinical applications.
    *   The feature alignment strategy could be generalized to other LDM-based tasks and potentially adapted for other network architectures.

**Justification for the Score:**

LEAF represents a solid contribution to the field of medical image segmentation. It's not a groundbreaking architectural innovation, but it provides a practical and demonstrably effective approach to improve LDM performance by addressing specific limitations of existing methods. The efficient design and strong experimental results warrant a score that reflects its potential for real-world impact. The weaknesses related to incremental improvement and limited architectural exploration prevent a higher score. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects the paper's merits and limitations.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Information Security Based on LLM Approaches: A Review
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18215v1)
- **Authors**: Chang Gong, Zhongwen Li, Xiaoqi Li
- **Abstract**: Information security is facing increasingly severe challenges, and traditional protection means are difficult to cope with complex and changing threats. In recent years, as an emerging intelligent technology, large language models (LLMs) have shown a broad application prospect in the field of information security. In this paper, we focus on the key role of LLM in information security, systematically review its application progress in malicious behavior prediction, network threat analysis, system vulnerability detection, malicious code identification, and cryptographic algorithm optimization, and explore its potential in enhancing security protection performance. Based on neural networks and Transformer architecture, this paper analyzes the technical basis of large language models and their advantages in natural language processing tasks. It is shown that the introduction of large language modeling helps to improve the detection accuracy and reduce the false alarm rate of security systems. Finally, this paper summarizes the current application results and points out that it still faces challenges in model transparency, interpretability, and scene adaptability, among other issues. It is necessary to explore further the optimization of the model structure and the improvement of the generalization ability to realize a more intelligent and accurate information security protection system.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Information Security Based on LLM Approaches: A Review" surveys the applications of large language models (LLMs) in various areas of information security, including malware detection, cybersecurity analysis (DDoS attack prediction and network security log analysis), security vulnerability detection, and cryptographic algorithm optimization. It discusses the technical foundations of LLMs, particularly the Transformer architecture, and highlights their advantages in natural language processing tasks. The review covers existing research and identifies the challenges and potential benefits of using LLMs to enhance security protection systems.  It concludes with a discussion of future directions focusing on improving model transparency, interpretability, generalization, and exploring extended cryptographic applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper is primarily a review, which inherently limits its novelty. However, the novelty lies in *synthesizing* the existing research on applying LLMs to information security in a comprehensive manner. While individual applications of LLMs in security are known, a consolidated overview like this offers value. The connections drawn between diverse security areas (malware, network analysis, vulnerability detection, crypto) through the lens of LLMs provide a new perspective. However, it doesn't present any completely novel application or technique. The identification of challenges and future research directions is standard for a review paper, but isn't groundbreaking on its own. The critical analysis of these challenges also isn't particularly insightful, covering familiar territory.

**Significance:** The significance stems from the potential impact LLMs can have on information security. By showcasing existing applications and highlighting areas for future research, the paper acts as a catalyst for further investigation in this domain. It demonstrates the potential of LLMs to improve detection accuracy, reduce false alarm rates, and automate security tasks. However, the paper's reliance on simply *summarizing* existing work weakens its significance. While it brings different applications together, it doesn't perform a significant critical analysis of the *efficacy* of the techniques, relying on others' reported results.  A deeper dive into the limitations and practical deployment challenges of each approach would have significantly boosted its value. The identification of future research directions, while useful, is fairly high-level. Specific research proposals, even at a conceptual level, would have been more impactful.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The paper provides a broad overview of LLM applications in various information security domains.
*   **Clear Explanations:** The paper explains the technical concepts of LLMs and their applications in security in an accessible manner.
*   **Identifies Key Challenges:** The paper accurately identifies challenges such as model transparency, interpretability, and generalization.
*   **Organized Structure:** The paper is well-structured and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Critical Analysis:** The paper primarily summarizes existing work and lacks a deep, critical analysis of the strengths and weaknesses of each approach.
*   **Lack of Novel Insights:** The paper does not present any novel applications or techniques.
*   **Superficial Discussion of Challenges:** The challenges and future research directions are identified at a high level, without specific proposals for addressing them.
*   **Limited Practical Considerations:** The paper lacks a thorough discussion of the practical deployment challenges of LLM-based security systems, such as computational costs, data requirements, and adversarial robustness.
*   **Limited Discussion of Ethical Implications:** The ethical implications of using LLMs in security, such as bias and privacy concerns, are not adequately addressed.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a moderately useful review. It is helpful in providing a starting point for researchers interested in the area. However, the lack of critical analysis, novel insights, and discussion of practical/ethical considerations limits its overall impact.  It's more of a high-level overview than a deep dive that pushes the field forward. It identifies *what* is being done, but falls short of answering *how well* it works and *what's next* in a truly compelling way.

Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 5/10

### GenAI for Automotive Software Development: From Requirements to Wheels
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18223v1)
- **Authors**: Nenad Petrovic, Fengjunjie Pan, Vahid Zolfaghari, Krzysztof Lebioda, Andre Schamschurko, Alois Knoll
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces a GenAI-empowered approach to automated development of automotive software, with emphasis on autonomous and Advanced Driver Assistance Systems (ADAS) capabilities. The process starts with requirements as input, while the main generated outputs are test scenario code for simulation environment, together with implementation of desired ADAS capabilities targeting hardware platform of the vehicle connected to testbench. Moreover, we introduce additional steps for requirements consistency checking leveraging Model-Driven Engineering (MDE). In the proposed workflow, Large Language Models (LLMs) are used for model-based summarization of requirements (Ecore metamodel, XMI model instance and OCL constraint creation), test scenario generation, simulation code (Python) and target platform code generation (C++). Additionally, Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) is adopted to enhance test scenario generation from autonomous driving regulations-related documents. Our approach aims shorter compliance and re-engineering cycles, as well as reduced development and testing time when it comes to ADAS-related capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a GenAI-driven workflow for automating automotive software development, focusing on ADAS features.  It leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) and Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) for tasks like requirements summarization, test scenario generation, and code generation (simulation and target platform). It also integrates Model-Driven Engineering (MDE) principles for requirements consistency checking. The workflow starts from requirements and culminates in test scenarios for simulation environments and code for the vehicle's hardware platform. The goal is to shorten compliance cycles, reduce development time, and improve ADAS capabilities development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the holistic integration of several existing techniques (LLMs, RAG, MDE) into a comprehensive end-to-end workflow specifically tailored to automotive software development.  While each of the individual components (using LLMs for code generation, RAG for regulatory compliance, MDE for consistency) has been explored in isolation, the combination and application to the automotive domain, especially encompassing the entire development lifecycle, represent a novel contribution. The iterative metamodeling approach using LLMs and PlantUML is also a notable contribution.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in the automotive industry: the increasing complexity and time-to-market pressures associated with developing advanced driver-assistance systems. By automating various stages of the development process, the proposed workflow has the potential to significantly reduce development time, costs, and errors. The emphasis on compliance with automotive regulations is also crucial, given the safety-critical nature of the domain. Furthermore, the focus on locally deployable AI models addresses concerns about exposing sensitive data. The use of MDE techniques to improve the trustworthiness of the LLM outputs directly responds to a major concern in the AI domain.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Workflow:** The paper presents a complete end-to-end workflow, covering all stages from requirements to code generation and testing.
*   **Integration of Techniques:**  It effectively integrates LLMs, RAG, and MDE to leverage their respective strengths.
*   **Focus on Automotive Domain:** The paper is specifically tailored to the automotive domain and addresses its unique challenges and regulatory requirements.
*   **Practical Considerations:** The consideration of locally deployable AI models demonstrates attention to practical deployment constraints.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The workflow is well-defined, and the steps are clearly explained.
*   **Hallucination Handling:** Addressed the trustworthiness concern by including hallucination tackling techniques.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Evaluation:** The paper lacks a detailed quantitative evaluation of the proposed workflow's effectiveness. While the authors mention initial results showing significant time reduction, more rigorous experimental validation (e.g., with real-world automotive projects, comparison to traditional methods) is needed to fully assess the impact.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** The paper does not address the scalability of the proposed workflow to larger, more complex automotive projects involving hundreds of thousands of requirements.
*   **Generalizability:** The approach seems tailored to specific ADAS features (automated emergency braking), and its generalizability to other automotive applications may be limited.
*   **Tooling Dependencies:** The reliance on specific tools (CARLA, n8n) may limit the portability and adoption of the workflow.
*   **Human-in-the-loop assumption:** A human reviewer in the loop is still required, and even if the need for manual intervention is reduced, the responsibility of final check stays.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the automotive software development field by demonstrating the feasibility of using GenAI to automate various stages of the development process. It could inspire further research into the application of LLMs and RAG for automotive engineering and lead to the development of more sophisticated and automated tools. The emphasis on compliance and safety will also be valuable for practitioners in the field.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel integration of existing techniques to address a real-world problem in the automotive industry. It has the potential to significantly reduce development time and costs. However, the lack of detailed quantitative evaluation and limitations in scalability and generalizability are significant drawbacks. Therefore, a score of 7 is appropriate, reflecting the paper's potential impact while acknowledging its limitations.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Assemble Your Crew: Automatic Multi-agent Communication Topology Design via Autoregressive Graph Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18224v1)
- **Authors**: Shiyuan Li, Yixin Liu, Qingsong Wen, Chengqi Zhang, Shirui Pan
- **Abstract**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) based on large language models (LLMs) have emerged as a powerful solution for dealing with complex problems across diverse domains. The effectiveness of MAS is critically dependent on its collaboration topology, which has become a focal point for automated design research. However, existing approaches are fundamentally constrained by their reliance on a template graph modification paradigm with a predefined set of agents and hard-coded interaction structures, significantly limiting their adaptability to task-specific requirements. To address these limitations, we reframe MAS design as a conditional autoregressive graph generation task, where both the system composition and structure are designed jointly. We propose ARG-Designer, a novel autoregressive model that operationalizes this paradigm by constructing the collaboration graph from scratch. Conditioned on a natural language task query, ARG-Designer sequentially and dynamically determines the required number of agents, selects their appropriate roles from an extensible pool, and establishes the optimal communication links between them. This generative approach creates a customized topology in a flexible and extensible manner, precisely tailored to the unique demands of different tasks. Extensive experiments across six diverse benchmarks demonstrate that ARG-Designer not only achieves state-of-the-art performance but also enjoys significantly greater token efficiency and enhanced extensibility. The source code of ARG-Designer is available at https://github.com/Shiy-Li/ARG-Designer.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Assemble Your Crew: Automatic Multi-agent Communication Topology Design via Autoregressive Graph Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of automatically designing effective communication topologies for Multi-Agent Systems (MAS) powered by Large Language Models (LLMs). Existing methods rely on modifying pre-defined template graphs, which limits their adaptability and scalability. The authors propose a novel approach called ARG-DESIGNER, which reframes the problem as a conditional autoregressive graph generation task. Instead of modifying a template, ARG-DESIGNER constructs the collaboration graph from scratch, iteratively selecting agent roles and establishing communication links based on a natural language task query. This generative approach aims to create customized topologies tailored to specific task requirements.  Experiments on six diverse benchmarks demonstrate superior performance, token efficiency, and extensibility compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in shifting the paradigm from template-based graph modification to autoregressive graph generation for MAS topology design. While autoregressive models have been used in graph generation before, their application to automatically designing communication structures for LLM-powered agents is a novel contribution.  The idea of dynamically selecting agents from an extensible pool, rather than pruning a fixed set, is also significant.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing importance of MAS in tackling complex problems. An effective communication topology is crucial for MAS performance.  The limitations of template-based methods hinder adaptability to new tasks and agents. The ARG-DESIGNER approach offers a more flexible and extensible solution, potentially enabling the development of more efficient and powerful MAS.  The improved token efficiency is also a valuable contribution, considering the high cost of running LLMs. The experimental results demonstrate substantial improvements across various benchmarks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Paradigm Shift:** The paper introduces a compelling paradigm shift in MAS topology design.
    *   **Extensibility:** ARG-DESIGNER's design allows easy integration of new agent roles without retraining.
    *   **Performance:** The experimental results consistently outperform existing methods, demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   **Token Efficiency:** ARG-DESIGNER generates more efficient communication structures, reducing the computational cost.
    *   **Robustness:** ARG-DESIGNER is more robust to prompt injection attacks.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The experiments are performed on multiple benchmarks with multiple strong baselines. The ablation study contributes to the understanding of different components of the model.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** Autoregressive graph generation can be computationally intensive, particularly for large graphs. The paper mitigates this through careful model design and a two-phase training strategy, but the scalability of the approach remains a concern for very large MAS.
    *   **Dependency on Data:** Like all machine learning models, ARG-DESIGNER relies on training data. While the authors propose a synthetic data generation strategy, the quality and diversity of the training data can significantly impact the performance of the model.
    *   **Black Box Design:** While the paper describes the overall design of ARG-DESIGNER, it would be valuable to gain more insight into the collaboration structures the model discovers and the rationale behind them. A deeper qualitative analysis could further enhance the understanding and trustworthiness of the approach.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM-powered MAS. The ARG-DESIGNER approach can enable the development of more adaptable, efficient, and robust MAS for a wide range of applications. The idea of autoregressive topology design could inspire further research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in the rapidly evolving field of LLM-powered MAS. The shift from template-based to autoregressive topology design is a valuable contribution, offering enhanced flexibility, scalability, and efficiency. The extensive experimental results and the robustness/extensibility analysis strongly support the effectiveness of ARG-DESIGNER. However, the potential scalability limitations and the reliance on synthetic data are valid concerns. A slightly higher score could have been justified with a more thorough qualitative analysis and discussion of potential limitations related to even larger multi-agent systems.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 8/10

### Multimodal Behavioral Patterns Analysis with Eye-Tracking and LLM-Based Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18252v1)
- **Authors**: Dongyang Guo, Yasmeen Abdrabou, Enkeleda Thaqi, Enkelejda Kasneci
- **Abstract**: Eye-tracking data reveals valuable insights into users' cognitive states but is difficult to analyze due to its structured, non-linguistic nature. While large language models (LLMs) excel at reasoning over text, they struggle with temporal and numerical data. This paper presents a multimodal human-AI collaborative framework designed to enhance cognitive pattern extraction from eye-tracking signals. The framework includes: (1) a multi-stage pipeline using horizontal and vertical segmentation alongside LLM reasoning to uncover latent gaze patterns; (2) an Expert-Model Co-Scoring Module that integrates expert judgment with LLM output to generate trust scores for behavioral interpretations; and (3) a hybrid anomaly detection module combining LSTM-based temporal modeling with LLM-driven semantic analysis. Our results across several LLMs and prompt strategies show improvements in consistency, interpretability, and performance, with up to 50% accuracy in difficulty prediction tasks. This approach offers a scalable, interpretable solution for cognitive modeling and has broad potential in adaptive learning, human-computer interaction, and educational analytics.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper presents a multimodal human-AI collaborative framework for analyzing eye-tracking data. This framework aims to improve the extraction of cognitive patterns from eye-tracking signals by combining the strengths of Large Language Models (LLMs) with expert knowledge and temporal modeling. The framework consists of three main modules: (1) a multi-stage pipeline for identifying latent gaze patterns through horizontal and vertical segmentation with LLM reasoning, (2) an Expert-Model Co-Scoring Module for evaluating the credibility of behavioral interpretations, and (3) a hybrid anomaly detection module that integrates LSTM-based temporal modeling with LLM-driven semantic analysis. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their framework in improving consistency, interpretability, and performance in tasks such as difficulty prediction, achieving up to 50% accuracy improvement. They argue that their approach offers a scalable and interpretable solution for cognitive modeling with potential applications in education, HCI, and educational analytics.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty and Significance:*

The core idea of combining eye-tracking data analysis with LLMs is promising, but the framework's novelty depends on how unique its individual components and their integration are.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a Real Problem:** Eye-tracking data analysis is complex, and LLMs struggle with numerical and temporal data. The paper tackles a crucial challenge in making eye-tracking analysis more accessible and interpretable.
    *   **Human-AI Collaboration:** The emphasis on combining human expertise with AI is a significant strength. This approach aims to create a more trustworthy and reliable system compared to relying solely on AI. The Expert-Model Co-Scoring module is a notable contribution in this regard.
    *   **Anomaly Detection with Semantic Context:** Integrating LSTM-based temporal modeling with LLM-driven semantic analysis for anomaly detection is a valuable approach. It allows for more context-aware and interpretable detection of unusual user behaviors.
    *   **Multi-Stage Analysis:** the framework uses multi-stage collaborative mechanism to achieve more precise and reliable outcomes.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The novelty of individual components might be limited. LLMs for data analysis and LSTM for time-series data are not novel in themselves. The key is in how these existing techniques are creatively combined and adapted. Further comparisons to alternative LLM-driven approaches for analyzing structured data are missing.
    *   **Evaluation Rigor:** The paper demonstrates improvements in consistency, interpretability, and performance. The specific evaluation metrics and their relevance to real-world applications should be discussed in detail. Also, more comprehensive comparison experiments with state-of-the-art baseline techniques will be helpful to demonstrate effectiveness.
    *   **Generalizability Concerns:** The experiments are primarily focused on programming learning tasks. The generalizability of the framework to other domains (e.g., medical diagnosis, user interface design) needs further investigation.
    *   **LLM Hallucinations and Biases:** The paper acknowledges potential biases and limitations but could delve deeper into mitigation strategies. In particular, methods to detect and correct LLM's "hallucinations" when interpreting numerical data should be discussed.
    *   **Lack of detailed information:** There are several pieces of important information that is missing. For example, the data cleaning process that is mentioned is quite vague. The evaluation metrics used for anomaly detection are not explicitly mentioned. The specific anomaly detection prompts for the LLM are not included.

*Significance:*

The work has the potential to impact several fields:

*   **Educational Analytics:** By providing a scalable and interpretable solution for analyzing student behavior, this framework could enable more personalized and effective learning experiences.
*   **Human-Computer Interaction:** The framework could be used to design more adaptive and user-friendly interfaces by understanding user attention and cognitive states in real-time.
*   **Cognitive Science:** The framework offers a valuable tool for researchers to study cognitive processes and build more sophisticated models of human behavior.

**Overall Assessment**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by proposing a human-AI collaborative framework for eye-tracking data analysis. While the novelty of individual components might be limited, the integration of LLMs, expert knowledge, and temporal modeling holds promise for improving the interpretability and scalability of cognitive pattern extraction. The evaluation section could be strengthened by including a more rigorous comparison with the state-of-the-art alternatives, and more information on each individual component in the experiments. The potential ethical implications and limitations of LLMs are addressed, but can be explored further in detail.

**Score: 6.5**

*Rationale:* The score reflects the work's potential but also acknowledges its limitations. The proposed framework addresses an important problem and combines several existing techniques to enhance the capabilities of LLMs. However, the novelty is incremental and would benefit from further improvements in the evaluation section. A more detailed justification of experimental designs would also strengthen the paper. The framework seems to have significance for different domains such as education, human-computer interaction, and cognitive science, and therefore further work with different experimental setups could improve the significance.

*Note:* This is my best judgement given the information available in the text. A more thorough assessment would require deeper knowledge and testing of the proposed framework.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Exploiting Gaussian Agnostic Representation Learning with Diffusion Priors for Enhanced Infrared Small Target Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18260v1)
- **Authors**: Junyao Li, Yahao Lu, Xingyuan Guo, Xiaoyu Xian, Tiantian Wang, Yukai Shi
- **Abstract**: Infrared small target detection (ISTD) plays a vital role in numerous practical applications. In pursuit of determining the performance boundaries, researchers employ large and expensive manual-labeling data for representation learning. Nevertheless, this approach renders the state-of-the-art ISTD methods highly fragile in real-world challenges. In this paper, we first study the variation in detection performance across several mainstream methods under various scarcity -- namely, the absence of high-quality infrared data -- that challenge the prevailing theories about practical ISTD. To address this concern, we introduce the Gaussian Agnostic Representation Learning. Specifically, we propose the Gaussian Group Squeezer, leveraging Gaussian sampling and compression for non-uniform quantization. By exploiting a diverse array of training samples, we enhance the resilience of ISTD models against various challenges. Then, we introduce two-stage diffusion models for real-world reconstruction. By aligning quantized signals closely with real-world distributions, we significantly elevate the quality and fidelity of the synthetic samples. Comparative evaluations against state-of-the-art detection methods in various scarcity scenarios demonstrate the efficacy of the proposed approach.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of infrared small target detection (ISTD) in scenarios where labeled training data is scarce. The authors propose a novel Gaussian Agnostic Representation Learning approach, which consists of three main components: a Gaussian Group Squeezer, a coarse-rebuilding stage, and a diffusion stage. The Gaussian Group Squeezer compresses the image using non-uniform quantization based on Gaussian sampling, generating a diverse set of training samples. The coarse-rebuilding and diffusion stages then enhance the quality and fidelity of these synthetic samples by aligning them with real-world distributions. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method improves detection accuracy and robustness, especially in few-shot learning scenarios, compared to existing state-of-the-art ISTD techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strength:** The core novelty lies in the integration of Gaussian-based quantization with diffusion models for data augmentation in the context of ISTD. While Gaussian sampling and diffusion models are not entirely new concepts, their specific application and combination within the proposed Gaussian Agnostic Representation Learning framework seem relatively novel, particularly in addressing the data scarcity problem in ISTD. The Gaussian Group Squeezer provides a novel way to generate diverse, yet controlled, variations of the input data, which is then refined by the diffusion models.
*   **Weakness:** The individual components (Gaussian sampling, diffusion models, autoencoders) are established techniques. The novelty is primarily in the specific configuration and application to this particular problem domain.  The incremental nature of the improvements must also be acknowledged.

**Significance:**

*   **Strength:** The significance stems from the potential to improve the performance of ISTD systems in real-world scenarios where obtaining large, high-quality labeled datasets is challenging. The demonstrated improvements in detection accuracy, especially in few-shot settings, are promising and could have practical implications for applications like video surveillance, early warning systems, and environmental monitoring. Furthermore, the approach provides a potential pathway toward reducing reliance on expensive and time-consuming manual labeling. The cross-domain generalizability is also a significant contribution.
*   **Weakness:** While the experimental results show improvements, it is important to acknowledge the limited scope of the datasets used (SIRST and NUDT-SIRST). The generalization to other, more diverse infrared datasets needs to be further investigated. The reliance on specific architectural choices (e.g., ResNet, Swin Transformer) for feature extraction and detection might also limit the broader applicability of the approach without further adaptation. The impact on real-time processing capabilities needs to be considered more explicitly.

**Specific Points to Consider:**

*   The paper clearly articulates the problem of data scarcity in ISTD and the limitations of existing methods.
*   The description of the Gaussian Agnostic Representation Learning framework and its components is generally clear and well-structured.
*   The experimental setup is well-defined, and the evaluation metrics are appropriate for the task.
*   The comparisons with state-of-the-art methods demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach, particularly in few-shot scenarios.
*   The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of individual components.
*   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational complexity and real-time performance implications of the proposed method.
*   Further investigation into the robustness of the approach to different types of noise and clutter in infrared images would also be valuable.
*   Discussion around the limitations of the approach should be more detailed, with suggested avenues for improvement.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, I assign the paper a score of **7**.

The paper presents a relatively novel and significant contribution to the field of ISTD by addressing the data scarcity problem with a well-designed and experimentally validated framework. The integration of Gaussian-based quantization with diffusion models for data augmentation is a promising approach. However, the incremental nature of the novelty (relying on existing components) and the limitations in the scope of the datasets used, as well as the discussion of computational complexity, prevent it from achieving a higher score. The potential impact on real-world applications is considerable, but further research is needed to fully realize that potential. The paper demonstrates promising results, especially under limited training sample conditions, making it a notable contribution to the current state-of-the-art.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReSem3D: Refinable 3D Spatial Constraints via Fine-Grained Semantic Grounding for Generalizable Robotic Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18262v1)
- **Authors**: Chenyu Su, Weiwei Shang, Chen Qian, Fei Zhang, Shuang Cong
- **Abstract**: Semantics-driven 3D spatial constraints align highlevel semantic representations with low-level action spaces, facilitating the unification of task understanding and execution in robotic manipulation. The synergistic reasoning of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) and Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) enables cross-modal 3D spatial constraint construction. Nevertheless, existing methods have three key limitations: (1) coarse semantic granularity in constraint modeling, (2) lack of real-time closed-loop planning, (3) compromised robustness in semantically diverse environments. To address these challenges, we propose ReSem3D, a unified manipulation framework for semantically diverse environments, leveraging the synergy between VFMs and MLLMs to achieve fine-grained visual grounding and dynamically constructs hierarchical 3D spatial constraints for real-time manipulation. Specifically, the framework is driven by hierarchical recursive reasoning in MLLMs, which interact with VFMs to automatically construct 3D spatial constraints from natural language instructions and RGB-D observations in two stages: part-level extraction and region-level refinement. Subsequently, these constraints are encoded as real-time optimization objectives in joint space, enabling reactive behavior to dynamic disturbances. Extensive simulation and real-world experiments are conducted in semantically rich household and sparse chemical lab environments. The results demonstrate that ReSem3D performs diverse manipulation tasks under zero-shot conditions, exhibiting strong adaptability and generalization. Code and videos at https://resem3d.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReSem3D, a novel robotic manipulation framework designed for diverse environments. It leverages Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) and Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) to create a hierarchical 3D spatial constraint model. This model operates in two stages: part-level constraint extraction and region-level refinement. The constraints are then formulated as cost functions for a real-time Model Predictive Path Integral (MPPI) controller within the Isaac Gym simulation platform.  A Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) framework, also driven by MLLM reasoning, is implemented for autonomous task decomposition and closed-loop execution. The system is evaluated in simulated and real-world environments (household and chemical lab) exhibiting zero-shot capabilities and strong adaptability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *hierarchical, refinable 3D spatial constraint modeling* approach driven by MLLMs and VFMs. While previous work has utilized MLLMs and VFMs for robotic manipulation, ReSem3D's specific two-stage refinement process to create *optimizable* spatial constraints sets it apart. The integration of these constraints into a real-time MPPI control loop using Isaac Gym is also a significant technical contribution, although MPPI control is not new in itself. The paper also contributes a MLLM-driven TAMP framework, which provides a comprehensive architecture for end-to-end autonomous task execution. However, the core concept of combining LLMs with geometric constraints for robotic tasks isn't entirely novel, lowering the novelty score somewhat.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in robotics: achieving robust and generalizable manipulation in unstructured environments. The ability to dynamically construct and refine spatial constraints from natural language instructions and visual observations is a valuable advancement. The system's zero-shot capabilities and demonstrated adaptability in both simulation and real-world scenarios are promising indicators of its practical applicability. By moving towards *optimizable* spatial constraints and away from pure task space planning, ReSem3D offers benefits in terms of stability and real-time performance. This addresses a known pain point in many current LLM-based robotic systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive System:** ReSem3D integrates perception, reasoning, planning, and control into a unified framework.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The results in both simulation and real-world environments are compelling, demonstrating the system's effectiveness and generalizability.
    *   **Well-defined Approach:** The two-stage constraint modeling and MPPI control are clearly explained.
    *   **Addresses Key Limitations:** The paper directly tackles the limitations of previous approaches, specifically coarse semantic granularity and lack of real-time adaptability.
    *   The use of MLLM-driven TAMP is a significant advancement for autonomous task decomposition.
    *   Zero-shot adaptation is a powerful ability showcased by ReSem3D.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** The system relies heavily on the performance of existing MLLMs and VFMs. Advances in these models will directly improve ReSem3D, but limitations in these models will also impact its performance.
    *   **Computational Complexity:** While MPPI is used for real-time control, the MLLM-driven constraint modeling and planning stages likely have significant computational demands, though the paper does not delve into this aspect. The paper does mention GPU-acceleration enabling parallelization, though this is insufficient in addressing computational demands.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:**  While there are comparisons with baselines, a more detailed ablation study on the components of the ReSem3D framework (e.g., comparing performance with and without region-level refinement) would strengthen the analysis. The paper also does not analyze why certain failure modes occur.
    *   **Lack of Deep Dive in TAPNext Implementation:** TAPNext is used to for dynamic disturbances, though there is little detail on how this is implemented in this particular framework.
    *   **Overly Ambitious Title:** While the paper addresses semantically diverse environments, the title seems overly confident and slightly misleading, as the results are not universally applicable across all environments.

*   **Potential Impact:** ReSem3D has the potential to significantly influence the field of robotic manipulation by providing a practical and generalizable framework for integrating semantic reasoning with real-time control. It could accelerate the development of robots capable of performing complex tasks in unstructured environments.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** ReSem3D is a well-executed system that introduces a valuable approach to robotic manipulation by combining refinable hierarchical 3D spatial constraints with real-time control. While it relies on existing models and could benefit from more detailed ablation studies and computational complexity analysis, the demonstrated performance and adaptability are impressive and justify a score of 7. The integration of MLLM for autonomous task planning is a powerful achievement that significantly raises the score of the paper. While some similar concepts were explored in the field previously, ReSem3D is one of the more comprehensive achievements and is well-written and well-structured overall.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### BadReasoner: Planting Tunable Overthinking Backdoors into Large Reasoning Models for Fun or Profit
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18305v1)
- **Authors**: Biao Yi, Zekun Fei, Jianing Geng, Tong Li, Lihai Nie, Zheli Liu, Yiming Li
- **Abstract**: Large reasoning models (LRMs) have emerged as a significant advancement in artificial intelligence, representing a specialized class of large language models (LLMs) designed to tackle complex reasoning tasks. The defining characteristic of LRMs lies in their extensive chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning capabilities. In this paper, we identify a previously unexplored attack vector against LRMs, which we term "overthinking backdoors". We advance this concept by proposing a novel tunable backdoor, which moves beyond simple on/off attacks to one where an attacker can precisely control the extent of the model's reasoning verbosity. Our attack is implemented through a novel data poisoning methodology. It pairs a tunable trigger-where the number of repetitions signals the desired intensity-with a correspondingly verbose CoT response. These responses are programmatically generated by instructing a teacher LLM to inject a controlled number of redundant refinement steps into a correct reasoning process. The approach preserves output correctness, which ensures stealth and establishes the attack as a pure resource-consumption vector. Extensive empirical results on various LRMs demonstrate that our method can reliably trigger a controllable, multi-fold increase in the length of the reasoning process, without degrading the final answer's correctness. Our source code is available at https://github.com/FZaKK/BadReasoner.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel attack vector against Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) called "overthinking backdoors."  The core idea is to create a tunable backdoor that, when triggered, causes the LRM to engage in excessively verbose reasoning without affecting the accuracy of the final answer. This is achieved through data poisoning, where a tunable trigger (a repeated keyword) is paired with programmatically generated verbose Chain-of-Thought (CoT) responses. The number of repetitions of the keyword controls the "intensity" of the overthinking, allowing for fine-grained control over resource consumption.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of this attack on various LRMs, showing it can reliably increase the length of the reasoning process without degrading answer correctness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a relatively novel attack vector. While backdoor attacks on ML models are not new, focusing specifically on the overthinking aspect of *reasoning* models and making it tunable is a solid contribution. The idea of controlling the level of verbosity through a trigger's strength is interesting and provides a new dimension to backdoor attacks. The programmable generation of CoT data is a clever way to implement the attack.
*   **Significance:** The attack has the potential to be significant because it targets a fundamental weakness of LRMs: their resource intensity. By increasing computational cost and latency without affecting accuracy, it creates a stealthy Denial-of-Service vulnerability. It's more subtle than simply injecting malicious code or data that causes incorrect answers.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of overthinking in LRMs as a security vulnerability.
    *   **Tunable Backdoor Concept:** The tunable backdoor is a well-defined and potentially powerful attack strategy. It moves beyond basic "on/off" backdoors to allow for more nuanced control.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides extensive empirical results on multiple LRMs, demonstrating the effectiveness of the attack in practice. The results are persuasive and support the authors' claims.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** This attack is very practical and can occur in real-world scenarios. The scenario that a 3rd-party, pre-trained model may have this backdoor is realistic and has important implications for the security of applications using LRMs.
    *   **Case Study and ablation study:** A case study is provided, showing a simple walkthrough of what the model is thinking, which demonstrates the effectiveness of the backdoor. Additionally, different trigger designs, number of poisoned samples, and defences have been proposed, which increases confidence in the research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Simplicity of Triggers:** The trigger design (repeating a keyword) is relatively simple and potentially detectable by sophisticated input filters. The authors acknowledge this in future works, but this limits its stealthiness.
    *   **Teacher Model Dependence:** The data poisoning process relies on a teacher LLM to generate verbose CoT responses. The quality and nature of these responses will significantly impact the effectiveness of the attack. A better understanding of how the teacher model influences the backdoored model's behavior would be helpful.
    *   **Limited Defense Analysis:** The authors touched on prompt-based defense as well as fine-tuning. However, they need to consider more defenses that are tailored to reasoning models (e.g., filtering based on reasoning length).
    *   **Realism of Attack Scale:**  While 300 poisoned samples may be practical, a clearer discussion around the cost to implement this back door (the compute costs as well as the required dataset) is needed.
*   **Impact:** This work could have a good impact on the field by raising awareness about the vulnerabilities of LRMs, and particularly the exploitation of overthinking behavior. This research can also lead to new defenses specifically designed to counter such attacks.

Overall, the paper presents a novel and potentially significant attack vector against LRMs. While there are limitations, the core ideas are well-developed, and the empirical results are convincing. This paper should spark further research on the security and robustness of reasoning models.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The score reflects the novelty and significance of the overthinking backdoor attack, as well as the solid empirical validation. However, the relatively simple trigger design and dependence on a teacher model limit the overall impact, leading to a score below 8. A higher score could be possible if they explore more complex triggers as well as better defenses against this attack.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### State of Health Estimation of Batteries Using a Time-Informed Dynamic Sequence-Inverted Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18320v1)
- **Authors**: Janak M. Patel, Milad Ramezankhani, Anirudh Deodhar, Dagnachew Birru
- **Abstract**: The rapid adoption of battery-powered vehicles and energy storage systems over the past decade has made battery health monitoring increasingly critical. Batteries play a central role in the efficiency and safety of these systems, yet they inevitably degrade over time due to repeated charge-discharge cycles. This degradation leads to reduced energy efficiency and potential overheating, posing significant safety concerns. Accurate estimation of a State of Health (SoH) of battery is therefore essential for ensuring operational reliability and safety. Several machine learning architectures, such as LSTMs, transformers, and encoder-based models, have been proposed to estimate SoH from discharge cycle data. However, these models struggle with the irregularities inherent in real-world measurements: discharge readings are often recorded at non-uniform intervals, and the lengths of discharge cycles vary significantly. To address this, most existing approaches extract features from the sequences rather than processing them in full, which introduces information loss and compromises accuracy. To overcome these challenges, we propose a novel architecture: Time-Informed Dynamic Sequence Inverted Transformer (TIDSIT). TIDSIT incorporates continuous time embeddings to effectively represent irregularly sampled data and utilizes padded sequences with temporal attention mechanisms to manage variable-length inputs without discarding sequence information. Experimental results on the NASA battery degradation dataset show that TIDSIT significantly outperforms existing models, achieving over 50% reduction in prediction error and maintaining an SoH prediction error below 0.58%. Furthermore, the architecture is generalizable and holds promise for broader applications in health monitoring tasks involving irregular time-series data.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel transformer-based architecture called Time-Informed Dynamic Sequence Inverted Transformer (TIDSIT) for estimating the State of Health (SoH) of lithium-ion batteries.  The core problem addressed is the challenge of dealing with irregular sampling intervals, variable sequence lengths, and limited historical context in real-world battery discharge data. TIDSIT overcomes these challenges by incorporating continuous-time embeddings, data variate embeddings (for voltage, current, and temperature), a temporal attention mechanism operating on padded sequences, and an optional SoH history embedding. The paper demonstrates that TIDSIT significantly outperforms existing models (including LSTMs, FNNs, and an i-Transformer variant) on a NASA battery degradation dataset, achieving a substantial reduction in prediction error. Ablation studies are used to show the contribution of each component of the TIDSIT architecture.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the specific combination of architectural components to handle the peculiarities of battery data.  While continuous-time embeddings and attention mechanisms are not new *per se*, their integration within an inverted transformer architecture *and* specifically tailored to the battery SoH problem appears to be a unique contribution.  The combination of temporal attention for handling padding, continuous-time embeddings for irregular sampling, and variate embeddings is a fairly innovative strategy to avoid reliance on feature engineering.  The SoH history embedding, while potentially useful, seems less crucial given the results of the ablation study.  The inverted transformer part of the architecture seems less novel than the time-informed components.

* **Significance:** The problem of accurate battery SoH estimation is of significant practical importance for electric vehicles, energy storage systems, and other applications.  The paper convincingly demonstrates that TIDSIT provides a considerable improvement in accuracy compared to existing methods *on the chosen dataset*.  The ability to directly process raw discharge data without feature engineering is a major advantage, reducing the dependence on domain expertise and potentially making the model more adaptable to different battery types and operating conditions. The results suggest that this model could have significant implications for battery management systems, allowing for more reliable and efficient operation.
However, the evaluation is limited to *one* dataset (albeit a commonly used one). Stronger support would be provided through generalizability analysis by either augmenting the NASA dataset or evaluating the model on a completely new dataset. This is an important caveat, as the benefits of TIDSIT could be dataset-dependent.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem definition and motivation.
    * Well-described architecture and components.
    * Strong experimental results showing significant improvement over baselines on the chosen dataset.
    * Thorough ablation study that identifies the key contributions of each component.
    * The ablations studies are well-conducted and informative.
    * The model avoids the need for handcrafted features, making it potentially more adaptable and generalizable.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Evaluation is limited to a single dataset.  More comprehensive evaluation on different datasets would strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    * Although the architecture is well-described, there could be more detail regarding implementation.
    * Although the ablation study is comprehensive in that it identifies the importance of each component, it would also be useful to see how the model components interact and/or compliment each other.
    * Some components may be dataset specific.

* **Potential Influence:** If the TIDSIT architecture proves to be robust across a wider range of battery types and operating conditions, it could have a significant impact on battery management systems. Its ability to directly process raw data could also inspire further research into end-to-end learning methods for other time-series problems.

* **Overall:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the field. However, the lack of generalizability analysis leaves some room for improvement. The novelty stems from the integration of time-aware mechanisms in the context of battery SoH, rather than revolutionizing the existing mechanisms outright. Taking this into account, the paper warrants a positive, albeit not stellar, score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### EgoExoBench: A Benchmark for First- and Third-person View Video Understanding in MLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18342v1)
- **Authors**: Yuping He, Yifei Huang, Guo Chen, Baoqi Pei, Jilan Xu, Tong Lu, Jiangmiao Pang
- **Abstract**: Transferring and integrating knowledge across first-person (egocentric) and third-person (exocentric) viewpoints is intrinsic to human intelligence, enabling humans to learn from others and convey insights from their own experiences. Despite rapid progress in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), their ability to perform such cross-view reasoning remains unexplored. To address this, we introduce EgoExoBench, the first benchmark for egocentric-exocentric video understanding and reasoning. Built from publicly available datasets, EgoExoBench comprises over 7,300 question-answer pairs spanning eleven sub-tasks organized into three core challenges: semantic alignment, viewpoint association, and temporal reasoning. We evaluate 13 state-of-the-art MLLMs and find that while these models excel on single-view tasks, they struggle to align semantics across perspectives, accurately associate views, and infer temporal dynamics in the ego-exo context. We hope EgoExoBench can serve as a valuable resource for research on embodied agents and intelligent assistants seeking human-like cross-view intelligence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the EgoExoBench paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EgoExoBench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the ability of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to understand and reason about videos from both egocentric (first-person) and exocentric (third-person) perspectives.  The benchmark comprises over 7,300 question-answer pairs across eleven sub-tasks, organized into three core challenges: semantic alignment, viewpoint association, and temporal reasoning.  The authors evaluate 13 state-of-the-art MLLMs on the benchmark and find that while these models perform well on single-view tasks, they struggle with cross-view reasoning. The paper argues that EgoExoBench can serve as a valuable resource for research on embodied agents and intelligent assistants requiring human-like cross-view intelligence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the benchmark's focus on *cross-view* video understanding. Existing video understanding benchmarks primarily focus on either egocentric or exocentric perspectives in isolation. By creating a benchmark that explicitly requires reasoning between these viewpoints, the paper addresses a significant gap in the evaluation of MLLMs. While there are ego-exo datasets available, the construction of a dedicated benchmark with carefully crafted QA pairs for cross-view reasoning is a contribution.

*   **Significance:** The ability to reason across different viewpoints is crucial for human-like intelligence and essential for embodied agents and human-robot collaboration.  The paper highlights a critical weakness in current MLLMs, demonstrating that their performance on single-view tasks doesn't translate to competence in cross-view scenarios.  The benchmark provides a concrete way to measure progress in this area and encourages the development of new architectures and training strategies. However, the paper shows that even models equipped with explicit reasoning capabilities struggle to interleave text-based reasoning with information from dual-view videos, indicating a need for new prompting or architectural techniques.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined tasks:** The sub-tasks are clearly defined and address specific aspects of cross-view reasoning (semantic alignment, spatial correspondence, and temporal understanding).
    *   **Diverse dataset:** The benchmark leverages multiple publicly available ego-exo datasets, ensuring a diverse range of environments and activities.
    *   **Rigorous QA construction:** The paper details a comprehensive QA construction pipeline, including automated and human quality assurance measures. The use of multiple QA construction strategies (annotation-derived, LLM-generated, and human-annotated) enhances the benchmark's coverage and difficulty.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The evaluation includes a wide range of MLLMs, from open-source to closed-source, providing a broad overview of the current state-of-the-art.
    *   **Human baseline:** Including a human performance baseline provides context for the model performance and reveals a significant gap.
    *   **Ablation Study:** Ablation study revealed the value of cross-perspective cues for better understanding actions and demonstrated its practical value.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** While diverse, the datasets used in the benchmark might not fully capture the complexity and variety of real-world ego-exo scenarios.
    *   **MCQ Format:** Although the multiple-choice question (MCQ) format enables reliable and scalable evaluation, it may not fully reflect the nuances of open-ended human dialogue and reasoning.
    *   **Chain of Thought Limitation**: CoT prompting does not improve the current result. It indicates that the models require something more than just text decomposition.
    *   **Small Human Evaluations:** The fact that only two graduate students completed human evaluations makes the results not representative.

*   **Potential Influence:** EgoExoBench has the potential to significantly influence research in embodied AI, robotics, and computer vision.  It provides a standardized evaluation framework for cross-view reasoning, which is a critical component of human-like intelligence. The benchmark will likely drive the development of new MLLM architectures and training techniques specifically designed to address the challenges of cross-view understanding.

**Justification of Score:**

EgoExoBench represents a valuable and timely contribution to the field. Its focus on cross-view video understanding is both novel and significant, addressing a crucial gap in the evaluation of MLLMs. The benchmark is well-designed, leveraging diverse datasets and a rigorous QA construction pipeline. However, the limitations related to dataset scope and the constraints of the MCQ format, together with the limited size of human evaluation, temper the overall impact.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### UniSegDiff: Boosting Unified Lesion Segmentation via a Staged Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18362v1)
- **Authors**: Yilong Hu, Shijie Chang, Lihe Zhang, Feng Tian, Weibing Sun, Huchuan Lu
- **Abstract**: The Diffusion Probabilistic Model (DPM) has demonstrated remarkable performance across a variety of generative tasks. The inherent randomness in diffusion models helps address issues such as blurring at the edges of medical images and labels, positioning Diffusion Probabilistic Models (DPMs) as a promising approach for lesion segmentation. However, we find that the current training and inference strategies of diffusion models result in an uneven distribution of attention across different timesteps, leading to longer training times and suboptimal solutions. To this end, we propose UniSegDiff, a novel diffusion model framework designed to address lesion segmentation in a unified manner across multiple modalities and organs.
