- **Authors**: Xinyuan Yan, Rita Sevastjanova, Sinie van der Ben, Mennatallah El-Assady, Bei Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) produce high-dimensional embeddings that capture rich semantic and syntactic relationships between words, sentences, and concepts. Investigating the topological structures of LLM embedding spaces via mapper graphs enables us to understand their underlying structures. Specifically, a mapper graph summarizes the topological structure of the embedding space, where each node represents a topological neighborhood (containing a cluster of embeddings), and an edge connects two nodes if their corresponding neighborhoods overlap. However, manually exploring these embedding spaces to uncover encoded linguistic properties requires considerable human effort. To address this challenge, we introduce a framework for semi-automatic annotation of these embedding properties. To organize the exploration process, we first define a taxonomy of explorable elements within a mapper graph such as nodes, edges, paths, components, and trajectories. The annotation of these elements is executed through two types of customizable LLM-based agents that employ perturbation techniques for scalable and automated analysis. These agents help to explore and explain the characteristics of mapper elements and verify the robustness of the generated explanations. We instantiate the framework within a visual analytics workspace and demonstrate its effectiveness through case studies. In particular, we replicate findings from prior research on BERT's embedding properties across various layers of its architecture and provide further observations into the linguistic properties of topological neighborhoods.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper, incorporating the specified requirements for rigor, critical assessment, and score justification:

**Summary:**

The paper "Explainable Mapper: Charting LLM Embedding Spaces Using Perturbation-Based Explanation and Verification Agents" introduces a framework for exploring and interpreting the topological structures of Large Language Model (LLM) embedding spaces.  It leverages mapper graphs – a topological data analysis technique – to visualize clusters and relationships within the high-dimensional embedding spaces. The core novelty lies in the introduction of "mapper agents," specifically Explainable Agents and Verification Agents, powered by LLMs themselves. These agents automate the annotation process by performing summarization, comparison, and perturbation operations on mapper elements (nodes, edges, paths).  The framework is instantiated within a visual analytics workspace (Explainable Mapper) to facilitate interactive exploration and hypothesis generation/verification. The authors demonstrate the framework's effectiveness through replication studies, re-examining known properties of BERT embeddings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Significant Problem:** Interpreting LLM embeddings is a crucial step towards understanding and controlling these powerful models. The paper tackles this challenge head-on.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The use of LLM-powered agents to automate the annotation of mapper graph elements is a creative and promising approach. It alleviates the burden of manual exploration and allows for scalable analysis.
    *   **Clear Taxonomy and Framework:** The paper clearly defines the elements of a mapper graph taxonomy suitable for LLM embedding analysis (nodes, edges, paths, etc.) and provides a structured framework for exploration.
    *   **Verification Mechanism:** The inclusion of Verification Agents is critical to address the potential for "hallucinations" in LLM-generated explanations. The perturbation-based approach offers a practical way to assess the robustness of interpretations.
    *   **Visual Analytics Workspace:** The Explainable Mapper workspace provides a valuable tool for users to interact with the embedding space, generate hypotheses, and verify explanations.
    *   **Replication Studies:** Demonstrating the ability to replicate existing findings in BERT embedding analysis lends credibility to the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The framework's performance is heavily dependent on the quality and reliability of the underlying LLMs. The possibility of hallucinations and biases remains a concern.
    *   **Perturbation Strategy:** The perturbation strategy (mainly 1-token changes) is relatively simple. More sophisticated perturbation methods might be needed to uncover subtle but significant embedding properties.
    *   **Trajectory Verification:** The manual verification of trajectory explanations represents a bottleneck in the automated exploration process.
    *   **Limited Generality:**  The replication studies are focused on BERT. While relevant, the framework's applicability and effectiveness across different LLM architectures and tasks requires further investigation.
    *   **Lack of Comparative Analysis:** The paper could be strengthened by comparing Explainable Mapper against existing embedding visualization or explanation tools, to better demonstrate its unique advantages.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper offers a unique combination of topological data analysis and LLM-powered automation to tackle the complex problem of interpreting LLM embeddings. The framework provides a more structured and scalable approach compared to purely manual exploration or traditional visualization techniques. While other works explore the topology of LLM spaces, or use LLMs for explanation, this paper's synergy of both makes for a novel approach.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The Explainable Mapper framework has the potential to become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working with LLMs. It could facilitate a deeper understanding of model behavior, identify biases, and improve model interpretability. This is important for responsible AI development.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a promising and novel approach, its reliance on LLMs and the limitations of the perturbation strategy prevent it from achieving a higher score. Further validation across different LLM architectures and tasks, and more sophisticated perturbation and verification methods, are needed to solidify its impact. Also, a comparative analysis is missing.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CG
- **Score**: 7/10

### TRPrompt: Bootstrapping Query-Aware Prompt Optimization from Textual Rewards
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18618v1)
- **Authors**: Andreea Nica, Ivan Zakazov, Nicolas Mario Baldwin, Saibo Geng, Robert West
- **Abstract**: Prompt optimization improves the reasoning abilities of large language models (LLMs) without requiring parameter updates to the target model. Following heuristic-based "Think step by step" approaches, the field has evolved in two main directions: while one group of methods uses textual feedback to elicit improved prompts from general-purpose LLMs in a training-free way, a concurrent line of research relies on numerical rewards to train a special prompt model, tailored for providing optimal prompts to the target model. In this paper, we introduce the Textual Reward Prompt framework (TRPrompt), which unifies these approaches by directly incorporating textual feedback into training of the prompt model. Our framework does not require prior dataset collection and is being iteratively improved with the feedback on the generated prompts. When coupled with the capacity of an LLM to internalize the notion of what a "good" prompt is, the high-resolution signal provided by the textual rewards allows us to train a prompt model yielding state-of-the-art query-specific prompts for the problems from the challenging math datasets GSMHard and MATH.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TRPrompt paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TRPrompt, a novel framework for optimizing prompts for large language models (LLMs), especially for math reasoning tasks. TRPrompt differentiates itself by using *textual rewards* rather than numerical rewards to guide the prompt optimization process.  This allows for a more nuanced and informative feedback loop.  The framework involves iteratively generating prompts, evaluating them using a reward model (generating textual critiques), fine-tuning the prompt model based on these textual rewards, and updating the notion of optimal reward using train-free techniques such as TextGrad.  The authors show state-of-the-art performance on challenging math datasets like GSMHard and MATH.  The framework is model-agnostic and doesn't require initial human-crafted prompts, starting from scratch.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *direct use of textual rewards for training the prompt model*. Previous methods typically rely on numerical rewards or use textual feedback in a train-free manner (e.g., updating prompts through iterative conversations with another LLM).  Bridging the gap between textual feedback and trainable prompt optimization is a significant contribution. The method of using textual reward in a trianing procedure that fine-tunes a language model towards generating prompts is a novel contribution. Further, the iterative self-improvement loop, leveraging textual rewards and avoiding reliance on initial expert prompts, also represents a distinct advance.
*   **Significance:** The work is significant because:
    *   *Textual rewards are more informative:* Numerical rewards are often sparse and fail to capture the subtleties of good prompting, especially in complex reasoning tasks. Textual rewards provide richer signals.
    *   *Overcomes numerical reward limitations:* In tasks where defining good numerical rewards is difficult (e.g., creative writing, poetry), textual rewards offer a viable alternative, expanding the applicability of prompt optimization.
    *   *Demonstrated Empirical Improvement:* The paper provides solid empirical evidence showing TRPrompt achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging benchmarks (GSMHard, MATH).
    *   *Model Agnostic & No Initial Prompt Dependency:* The ability to bootstrap prompts from scratch is highly desirable, reducing reliance on human expertise and potentially uncovering novel prompting strategies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Clear Problem Definition:* The paper clearly articulates the query-dependent prompt optimization problem and the limitations of existing solutions.
    *   *Well-Defined Framework:* TRPrompt is well-designed and modular. The iterative approach allows for continuous improvement.
    *   *Comprehensive Experiments:* The experiments are thorough and cover various aspects, including ablation studies (examining the impact of each step), cross-dataset generalization, and comparison with strong baselines.
    *   *Strong Results:* The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of TRPrompt, particularly on challenging datasets.
    *   *Solid Ablation insights:* Understanding the significance of different elements in our pipeline, will help future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Computational Cost:* The Textgrad step (optimal reward search) is computationally expensive, limiting scalability. The reliance on GPT-4o-mini for the optimal reward is a potential limitation. This also makes the experiments less readily reproducible and more dependent on API access.
    *   *Limited Improvement on Simpler Datasets:* The gains on GSM8K are modest, which the authors attribute to an unbalanced training set with mostly positive feedback. This suggests that TRPrompt might benefit from more sophisticated strategies for handling simpler tasks, such as curating a more balanced dataset or using a different reward model.
    *   *Generality of Textual Rewards:* While the paper argues for the generality of textual rewards, the experiments primarily focus on mathematical reasoning. Further investigation into other domains would strengthen the argument.
    *   *Lack of Theoretical Analysis:*  While empirically strong, the paper lacks a formal theoretical analysis of the convergence properties of the iterative training loop or the representational power of textual rewards.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The idea of using textual rewards for training prompt models is likely to inspire further research in this area.
    *   TRPrompt could become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working on LLMs, especially for tasks requiring complex reasoning.
    *   The iterative self-improvement framework could be adapted to other areas of LLM research, such as instruction following or model alignment.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **8** out of 10.

*   The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of prompt optimization by introducing the direct use of textual rewards for training. The empirical results are compelling, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on challenging benchmarks. The model-agnostic framework and the ability to bootstrap from scratch are also valuable features.

*   However, the high computational cost and the dependency on GPT-4o-mini raise concerns about scalability and reproducibility. The limited improvement on simpler datasets and the lack of a theoretical analysis also detract from the overall score. Further work is needed to address these limitations and to explore the generality of TRPrompt in other domains. While the core idea is novel and the empirical results are strong, the computational limitations and the lack of theoretical grounding prevent it from reaching a higher score.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Captain Cinema: Towards Short Movie Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18634v1)
- **Authors**: Junfei Xiao, Ceyuan Yang, Lvmin Zhang, Shengqu Cai, Yang Zhao, Yuwei Guo, Gordon Wetzstein, Maneesh Agrawala, Alan Yuille, Lu Jiang
- **Abstract**: We present Captain Cinema, a generation framework for short movie generation. Given a detailed textual description of a movie storyline, our approach firstly generates a sequence of keyframes that outline the entire narrative, which ensures long-range coherence in both the storyline and visual appearance (e.g., scenes and characters). We refer to this step as top-down keyframe planning. These keyframes then serve as conditioning signals for a video synthesis model, which supports long context learning, to produce the spatio-temporal dynamics between them. This step is referred to as bottom-up video synthesis. To support stable and efficient generation of multi-scene long narrative cinematic works, we introduce an interleaved training strategy for Multimodal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiT), specifically adapted for long-context video data. Our model is trained on a specially curated cinematic dataset consisting of interleaved data pairs. Our experiments demonstrate that Captain Cinema performs favorably in the automated creation of visually coherent and narrative consistent short movies in high quality and efficiency. Project page: https://thecinema.ai
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Captain Cinema, a framework for generating short movies from textual descriptions. The approach employs a two-stage process: 1) a "top-down" keyframe planning module that generates a sequence of representative frames to outline the narrative and ensure coherence in both storyline and visual appearance, and 2) a "bottom-up" video synthesis module that uses these keyframes as conditioning signals to generate the spatio-temporal dynamics and visual details between them.  A key component is GoldenMem, a memory mechanism that selectively retains and compresses contextual information from past keyframes. The model is trained using an interleaved training strategy on a curated cinematic dataset. The authors demonstrate through experiments that Captain Cinema can generate visually coherent and narrative-consistent short movies with high quality and efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several existing ideas into a complete pipeline. The two-stage approach (keyframe planning followed by video synthesis) is not entirely new, but the specific instantiation using multimodal diffusion transformers (MM-DiT), the GoldenMem compression technique, and the interleaved training strategy contributes a unique configuration. The dynamic stride sampling and progressive long-context fine-tuning are useful practical training enhancements. The application of these techniques to generate longer, multi-scene videos is where the main novelty exists.

*   **Significance:** The field of text-to-video generation has made substantial progress in creating short, high-fidelity clips, but generating longer, narrative-driven content remains a challenge. Captain Cinema takes a significant step toward addressing this challenge by focusing on long-range coherence and visual consistency. The ability to generate short films directly from text descriptions opens up possibilities for automated content creation and personalized storytelling. The reported qualitative results look impressive, and the inclusion of user studies and quantitative metrics lends credibility to the claims.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The modular design allows for independent improvements in each stage (keyframe planning and video synthesis).
    *   The GoldenMem mechanism is a clever way to manage long-range dependencies without excessive memory consumption.
    *   The experimental results show compelling qualitative and quantitative improvements over existing methods, particularly in temporal dynamics and narrative coherence.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of the different components and training strategies.
    *   The generalization studies (creative scene generation, cross-movie character swapping) highlight the model's flexibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dependence on external prompts (from LLMs) limits the autonomy of the system. The method can't conceive original stories.
    *   The data hunger is a significant concern. The availability of high-quality, feature-length movie datasets is limited. Real-world validation and architectural refinements are needed.
    *   While GoldenMem is efficient, it still imposes a compute overhead. Alternatives should be explored.
    *   The narrative coherence metrics used are largely based on automated evaluation using Gemini 2.5, which might not fully capture human perception of story quality. User study results for narrative coherence would have been useful.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper could inspire future research directions in several ways:
    *   Developing more sophisticated memory mechanisms for managing long-range dependencies in video generation.
    *   Exploring different architectures for keyframe planning and video synthesis.
    *   Creating better evaluation metrics for assessing narrative coherence in generated videos.
    *   Investigating ways to make text-to-video generation systems more autonomous in terms of story creation.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of text-to-video generation. The combination of techniques into a working system for generating longer, more complex video content is valuable. The quantitative and qualitative results provide evidence of the method's effectiveness.  The strengths of the paper (modular design, GoldenMem, comprehensive evaluation) outweigh the weaknesses (dependence on external prompts, data hunger). However, the lack of full autonomy in story creation and the reliance on automated narrative coherence metrics prevent a higher score. Further work is needed to address these limitations and fully realize the potential of this approach.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
